1→"""Context building for review: ReviewContext, shared helpers, heuristic signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from collections import Counter
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import (
    10→    disable_file_cache,
    11→    enable_file_cache,
    12→    is_file_cache_enabled,
    13→    read_file_text,
    14→    rel,
    15→    resolve_path,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import ReviewContext
    18→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    19→    CLASS_NAME_RE,
    20→    ERROR_PATTERNS,
    21→    FUNC_NAME_RE,
    22→    NAME_PREFIX_RE,
    23→    default_review_module_patterns,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.ai import gather_ai_debt_signals
    26→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.auth import gather_auth_context
    27→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.migration import (
    28→    classify_error_strategy,
    29→)
    30→
    31→# ── Shared helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    32→
    33→
    34→def abs_path(filepath: str) -> str:
    35→    """Resolve filepath to absolute using resolve_path."""
    36→    return resolve_path(filepath)
    37→
    38→
    39→def file_excerpt(filepath: str, max_lines: int = 30) -> str | None:
    40→    """Read first *max_lines* of a file, returning the text or None."""
    41→    content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
    42→    if content is None:
    43→        return None
    44→    lines = content.splitlines(keepends=True)
    45→    if len(lines) <= max_lines:
    46→        return content
    47→    return "".join(lines[:max_lines]) + f"\n... ({len(lines) - max_lines} more lines)"
    48→
    49→
    50→def dep_graph_lookup(
    51→    graph: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
    52→    filepath: str,
    53→) -> dict[str, object]:
    54→    """Look up a file in the dep graph, trying absolute and relative keys."""
    55→    resolved = resolve_path(filepath)
    56→    entry = graph.get(resolved)
    57→    if entry is not None:
    58→        return entry
    59→    # Try relative path
    60→    rpath = rel(filepath)
    61→    entry = graph.get(rpath)
    62→    if entry is not None:
    63→        return entry
    64→    return {}
    65→
    66→
    67→def importer_count(entry: dict) -> int:
    68→    """Extract importer count from a dep graph entry."""
    69→    importers = entry.get("importers", set())
    70→    if isinstance(importers, set):
    71→        return len(importers)
    72→    return entry.get("importer_count", 0)
    73→
    74→
    75→# ── Per-file review context builder ──────────────────────────────
    76→
    77→
    78→def build_review_context(
    79→    path: Path,
    80→    lang,
    81→    state: dict,
    82→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    83→) -> ReviewContext:
    84→    """Gather codebase conventions for contextual evaluation.
    85→
    86→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
    87→    """
    88→    if files is None:
    89→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    90→    ctx = ReviewContext()
    91→
    92→    if not files:
    93→        return ctx
    94→
    95→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
    96→    if not already_cached:
    97→        enable_file_cache()
    98→    try:
    99→        return _build_review_context_inner(files, lang, state, ctx)
   100→    finally:
   101→        if not already_cached:
   102→            disable_file_cache()
   103→
   104→
   105→def _build_review_context_inner(
   106→    files: list[str],
   107→    lang,
   108→    state: dict,
   109→    ctx: ReviewContext,
   110→) -> ReviewContext:
   111→    """Inner context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
   112→    # Pre-read all file contents once (cache will store them)
   113→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
   114→    for filepath in files:
   115→        content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   116→        if content is not None:
   117→            file_contents[filepath] = content
   118→
   119→    # 1. Naming vocabulary — extract function/class names, count prefixes
   120→    prefix_counter: Counter = Counter()
   121→    total_names = 0
   122→    for content in file_contents.values():
   123→        for name in FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content) + CLASS_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   124→            total_names += 1
   125→            match = NAME_PREFIX_RE.match(name)
   126→            if match:
   127→                prefix_counter[match.group(1)] += 1
   128→    ctx.naming_vocabulary = {
   129→        "prefixes": dict(prefix_counter.most_common(20)),
   130→        "total_names": total_names,
   131→    }
   132→
   133→    # 2. Error handling conventions — scan for patterns
   134→    error_counts: Counter = Counter()
   135→    for content in file_contents.values():
   136→        for pattern_name, pattern in ERROR_PATTERNS.items():
   137→            if pattern.search(content):
   138→                error_counts[pattern_name] += 1
   139→    ctx.error_conventions = dict(error_counts)
   140→
   141→    # 3. Module patterns — what each directory typically uses
   142→    dir_patterns: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   143→    module_pattern_fn = getattr(lang, "review_module_patterns_fn", None)
   144→    if not callable(module_pattern_fn):
   145→        module_pattern_fn = default_review_module_patterns
   146→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   147→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   148→        if len(parts) < 2:
   149→            continue
   150→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   151→        counter = dir_patterns.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   152→        pattern_names = module_pattern_fn(content)
   153→        if not isinstance(pattern_names, list | tuple | set):
   154→            pattern_names = default_review_module_patterns(content)
   155→        for pattern_name in pattern_names:
   156→            counter[pattern_name] += 1
   157→        if re.search(r"\bclass\s+\w+", content):
   158→            counter["class_based"] += 1
   159→    ctx.module_patterns = {
   160→        d: dict(c.most_common(3))
   161→        for d, c in dir_patterns.items()
   162→        if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   163→    }
   164→
   165→    # 4. Import graph summary — top files by importer count
   166→    if lang.dep_graph:
   167→        graph = lang.dep_graph
   168→        importer_counts = {}
   169→        for filepath, entry in graph.items():
   170→            count = importer_count(entry)
   171→            if count > 0:
   172→                importer_counts[rel(filepath)] = count
   173→        top = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])[:20]
   174→        ctx.import_graph_summary = {"top_imported": dict(top)}
   175→
   176→    # 5. Zone distribution
   177→    if lang.zone_map is not None:
   178→        ctx.zone_distribution = lang.zone_map.counts()
   179→
   180→    # 6. Existing findings per file (summaries only), scoped to active review files.
   181→    allowed_review_files = {
   182→        rel(filepath)
   183→        for filepath in file_contents
   184→        if isinstance(filepath, str) and filepath
   185→    }
   186→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   187→    by_file: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   188→    for finding in findings.values():
   189→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
   190→            continue
   191→        finding_file_raw = finding.get("file", "")
   192→        if not isinstance(finding_file_raw, str) or not finding_file_raw:
   193→            continue
   194→        finding_file = rel(finding_file_raw)
   195→        if finding_file not in allowed_review_files:
   196→            continue
   197→        by_file.setdefault(finding_file, []).append(
   198→            f"{finding['detector']}: {finding['summary'][:80]}"
   199→        )
   200→    ctx.existing_findings = by_file

1→"""Post-scan plan reconciliation — handle finding churn."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SupersededEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX
    10→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    11→
    12→
    13→SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS = 90
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class ReconcileResult:
    18→    """Summary of changes made during reconciliation."""
    19→
    20→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    21→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    22→    resurfaced: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    23→    changes: int = 0
    24→
    25→
    26→def _find_candidates(
    27→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    28→) -> list[str]:
    29→    """Find open findings that could be remaps for a disappeared finding."""
    30→    candidates: list[str] = []
    31→    for fid, finding in state.get("findings", {}).items():
    32→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    33→            continue
    34→        if finding.get("detector") == detector and finding.get("file") == file:
    35→            candidates.append(fid)
    36→    return candidates
    37→
    38→
    39→def _is_finding_alive(state: StateModel, finding_id: str) -> bool:
    40→    """Return True if the finding exists and is open."""
    41→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    42→    if finding is None:
    43→        return False
    44→    return finding.get("status") == "open"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _supersede_id(
    48→    plan: PlanModel,
    49→    state: StateModel,
    50→    finding_id: str,
    51→    now: str,
    52→) -> bool:
    53→    """Move a disappeared finding to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    54→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    55→    detector = ""
    56→    file = ""
    57→    summary = ""
    58→    if finding:
    59→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    60→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    61→        summary = finding.get("summary", "")
    62→
    63→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    64→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    65→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != finding_id]
    66→
    67→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    68→        "original_id": finding_id,
    69→        "original_detector": detector,
    70→        "original_file": file,
    71→        "original_summary": summary,
    72→        "status": "superseded",
    73→        "superseded_at": now,
    74→        "remapped_to": None,
    75→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    # Preserve any existing override note
    79→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(finding_id)
    80→    if override and override.get("note"):
    81→        entry["note"] = override["note"]
    82→
    83→    plan["superseded"][finding_id] = entry
    84→
    85→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, cluster finding_ids
    86→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    87→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    88→    if finding_id in order:
    89→        order.remove(finding_id)
    90→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    91→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    92→        ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
    93→        if finding_id in ids:
    94→            ids.remove(finding_id)
    95→
    96→    return True
    97→
    98→
    99→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   100→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   101→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   102→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   103→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   104→
   105→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   106→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   107→        try:
   108→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   109→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   110→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   111→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   112→                to_prune.append(fid)
   113→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   114→            to_prune.append(fid)
   115→
   116→    for fid in to_prune:
   117→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   118→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   119→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   120→
   121→    return to_prune
   122→
   123→
   124→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   125→    plan: PlanModel,
   126→    state: StateModel,
   127→) -> ReconcileResult:
   128→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   129→
   130→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   131→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   132→    """
   133→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   134→    result = ReconcileResult()
   135→    now = utc_now()
   136→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   137→
   138→    # Collect all finding IDs referenced by the plan
   139→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   140→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   141→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   142→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   143→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   144→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   145→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   146→
   147→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   148→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   149→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   150→    referenced_ids = {
   151→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   152→        if not fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   153→    }
   154→
   155→    # Check each referenced ID
   156→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   157→        if not _is_finding_alive(state, fid):
   158→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   159→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   160→                result.changes += 1
   161→
   162→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   163→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   164→    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import resurface_stale_skips
   165→
   166→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   167→    if resurfaced:
   168→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   169→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   170→
   171→    # Prune old superseded entries
   172→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   173→    result.pruned = pruned
   174→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   175→
   176→    return result
   177→
   178→
   179→__all__ = [
   180→    "ReconcileResult",
   181→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   182→]
   183→
```

    66→def _consume_path_override(name: str) -> Path | None:
    67→    """Consume one compatibility override to prevent cross-command bleed-through."""
    68→    value = globals().get(name)
    69→    if isinstance(value, Path):
    70→        globals()[name] = None
    71→        return value
    72→    return None

    75→def _resolve_batch_paths() -> BatchPathContext:

   173→        include_scan_after_import=False,

   175→    write_query_best_effort(
   176→        packet,
   177→        context="review packet query update",
   178→    )
   179→    packet_path, blind_saved = runner_helpers_mod.write_packet_snapshot(
   180→        packet,
   181→        stamp=stamp,
   182→        review_packet_dir=paths.review_packet_dir,
   183→        blind_path=blind_path,
   184→        safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   185→    )
   186→    print(colorize(f"  Immutable packet: {packet_path}", "dim"))
   187→    print(colorize(f"  Blind packet: {blind_saved}", "dim"))
   188→    return packet, packet_path, blind_saved
   189→
   190→
   191→def _do_run_batches(args, state, lang, state_file, config: dict | None = None) -> None:
   192→    """Run holistic investigation batches with a local subagent runner."""
   193→    paths = _resolve_batch_paths()
   194→    runtime_project_root = paths.project_root
   195→    batch_timeout_seconds = coerce_positive_int(
   196→        getattr(args, "batch_timeout_seconds", None),
   197→        default=CODEX_BATCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   198→        minimum=1,
   199→    )
   200→    batch_max_retries = coerce_positive_int(
   201→        getattr(args, "batch_max_retries", None),
   202→        default=1,
   203→        minimum=0,
   204→    )
   205→    batch_retry_backoff_seconds = coerce_non_negative_float(
   206→        getattr(args, "batch_retry_backoff_seconds", None),
   207→        default=2.0,
   208→    )
   209→    batch_heartbeat_seconds = coerce_non_negative_float(
   210→        getattr(args, "batch_heartbeat_seconds", None),
   211→        default=15.0,
   212→    )
   213→    batch_live_log_interval_seconds = (
   214→        max(1.0, min(batch_heartbeat_seconds, 10.0))
   215→        if batch_heartbeat_seconds > 0
   216→        else 5.0
   217→    )
   218→    batch_stall_kill_seconds = coerce_non_negative_int(
   219→        getattr(args, "batch_stall_kill_seconds", None),
   220→        default=CODEX_BATCH_STALL_KILL_SECONDS,
   221→    )
   222→
   223→    deps = review_batches_mod.BatchRunDependencies(
   224→        load_or_prepare_packet_fn=(
   225→            lambda batch_args, *, state, lang, config, stamp: _load_or_prepare_packet(
   226→                batch_args,
   227→                state=state,
   228→                lang=lang,
   229→                config=config,
   230→                stamp=stamp,
   231→                paths=paths,
   232→            )
   233→        ),
   234→        run_codex_batch_fn=(
   235→            lambda *, prompt, repo_root, output_file, log_file: runner_helpers_mod.run_codex_batch(
   236→                prompt=prompt,
   237→                repo_root=repo_root,
   238→                output_file=output_file,
   239→                log_file=log_file,
   240→                deps=runner_helpers_mod.CodexBatchRunnerDeps(
   241→                    timeout_seconds=batch_timeout_seconds,
   242→                    subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   243→                    timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   244→                    safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   245→                    use_popen_runner=(getattr(subprocess.run, "__module__", "") == "subprocess"),
   246→                    subprocess_popen=subprocess.Popen,
   247→                    live_log_interval_seconds=batch_live_log_interval_seconds,
   248→                    stall_after_output_seconds=batch_stall_kill_seconds,
   249→                    max_retries=batch_max_retries,
   250→                    retry_backoff_seconds=batch_retry_backoff_seconds,
   251→                ),
   252→            )
   253→        ),
   254→        merge_batch_results_fn=_merge_batch_results,
   255→        do_import_fn=_do_import,
   256→        run_followup_scan_fn=(
   257→            lambda *, lang_name, scan_path: runner_helpers_mod.run_followup_scan(
   258→                lang_name=lang_name,
   259→                scan_path=scan_path,
   260→                deps=runner_helpers_mod.FollowupScanDeps(

   262→                    timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   263→                    python_executable=sys.executable,
   264→                    subprocess_run=subprocess.run,

   266→                    colorize_fn=colorize,

   268→            )

   273→        subagent_runs_dir=paths.subagent_runs_dir,
   274→        abstraction_sub_axes=ABSTRACTION_SUB_AXES,
   275→    )
   276→    return review_batches_mod.do_run_batches(

1→"""Holistic investigation batch builders for review preparation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import HolisticContext
     8→
     9→_EXTENSIONLESS_FILENAMES = {
    10→    "makefile",
    11→    "dockerfile",
    12→    "readme",
    13→    "license",
    14→    "build",
    15→    "workspace",
    16→}
    17→
    18→_GOVERNANCE_REFERENCE_FILES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    19→    "README.md",
    20→    "DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md",
    21→    "desloppify/README.md",
    22→    "pyproject.toml",
    23→)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _normalize_file_path(value: object) -> str | None:
    27→    """Normalize/validate candidate file paths for batch payloads."""
    28→    if not isinstance(value, str):
    29→        return None
    30→    text = value.strip().strip(",'\"")
    31→    if not text or text in {".", ".."}:
    32→        return None
    33→    if text.endswith("/"):
    34→        return None
    35→
    36→    basename = Path(text).name
    37→    if not basename:
    38→        return None
    39→    if "." not in basename and basename.lower() not in _EXTENSIONLESS_FILENAMES:
    40→        return None
    41→    return text
    42→
    43→
    44→def _collect_unique_files(
    45→    sources: list[list[dict]],
    46→    key: str = "file",
    47→    *,
    48→    max_files: int | None = None,
    49→) -> list[str]:
    50→    """Collect unique file paths from multiple source lists."""
    51→    seen: set[str] = set()
    52→    out: list[str] = []
    53→    for src in sources:
    54→        for item in src:
    55→            f = _normalize_file_path(item.get(key, ""))
    56→            if f and f not in seen:
    57→                seen.add(f)
    58→                out.append(f)
    59→                if max_files is not None and len(out) >= max_files:
    60→                    return out
    61→    return out
    62→
    63→
    64→def _existing_repo_files(
    65→    repo_root: Path | None,
    66→    candidates: tuple[str, ...],
    67→) -> list[str]:
    68→    """Return repository-relative paths for candidate files that exist."""
    69→    if repo_root is None:
    70→        return []
    71→    out: list[str] = []
    72→    for candidate in candidates:
    73→        if (repo_root / candidate).is_file():
    74→            out.append(candidate)
    75→    return out
    76→
    77→
    78→def _collect_files_from_batches(
    79→    batches: list[dict], *, max_files: int | None = None
    80→) -> list[str]:
    81→    """Collect unique file paths across batch payloads (preserving order)."""
    82→    seen: set[str] = set()
    83→    out: list[str] = []
    84→    for batch in batches:
    85→        for filepath in batch.get("files_to_read", []):
    86→            normalized = _normalize_file_path(filepath)
    87→            if not normalized:
    88→                continue
    89→            if normalized in seen:
    90→                continue
    91→            seen.add(normalized)
    92→            out.append(normalized)
    93→            if max_files is not None and len(out) >= max_files:
    94→                return out
    95→    return out
    96→
    97→

    99→    ctx: HolisticContext,
   100→    directory: str,

    24→
    25→def _plan_path_for_runtime(state_path: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    26→    if state_path is None:
    27→        return None
    28→    return plan_path_for_state(state_path)
    29→
    30→
    31→def _persist_plan_and_state(
    32→    *,
    33→    plan_before: dict,
    34→    plan_after: dict,
    35→    plan_path: Path | None,
    36→    state_after: dict | None = None,
    37→    state_file: Path | None = None,
    38→) -> None:
    39→    """Persist plan/state as one operation and rollback plan on state-write failure."""
    40→    save_plan(plan_after, plan_path)
    41→    if state_after is None or state_file is None:
    42→        return
    43→
    44→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
    45→
    46→    try:
    47→        state_mod.save_state(state_after, state_file)
    48→    except Exception:
    49→        try:
    50→            save_plan(plan_before, plan_path)
    51→        except Exception as rollback_exc:
    52→            print(
    53→                colorize(
    54→                    "  Warning: failed to rollback plan after state write error: "
    55→                    f"{rollback_exc}",
    56→                    "yellow",
    57→                ),
    58→                file=sys.stderr,
    59→            )
    60→        raise
    61→
    62→
    63→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:

    76→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
    77→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    78→    if not finding_ids:

    80→        return
    81→
    82→    for fid in finding_ids:

   113→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   114→# Skip / unskip

   116→
   117→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   118→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
   119→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
   120→        show_attestation_requirement,
   121→        validate_attestation,
   122→    )
   123→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
   124→
   125→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   126→    state = runtime.state
   127→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   128→        return
   129→    plan_path = _plan_path_for_runtime(runtime.state_path)
   130→
   131→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   132→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
   133→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
   134→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
   135→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
   136→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   137→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   138→
   139→    # Determine skip kind
   140→    if false_positive:
   141→        kind = "false_positive"
   142→    elif permanent:
   143→        kind = "permanent"
   144→    else:
   145→        kind = "temporary"
   146→
   147→    # Validate requirements for permanent/false_positive
   148→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   149→        if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   150→            show_attestation_requirement(
   151→                "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
   152→                attestation,
   153→                ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
   154→            )
   155→            return
   156→        if kind == "permanent" and not note:
   157→            print(
   158→                colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
   159→                file=sys.stderr,
   160→            )
   161→            return
   162→
   163→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   164→    plan_before = deepcopy(plan)
   165→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   166→    if not finding_ids:
   167→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   168→        return
   169→
   170→    # For permanent/false_positive: delegate to state layer for score impact
   171→    resolved: list[str] = []
   172→    state_data: dict | None = None
   173→    state_file: Path | None = None
   174→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   175→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   176→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   177→
   178→        state_file = state_path(args)
   179→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   180→        status = "wontfix" if kind == "permanent" else "false_positive"
   181→        for fid in finding_ids:
   182→            resolved.extend(
   183→                state_mod.resolve_findings(
   184→                    state_data, fid, status, note or "", attestation=attestation
   185→                )
   186→            )
   187→
   188→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   189→    count = skip_items(
   190→        plan,
   191→        finding_ids,
   192→        kind=kind,
   193→        reason=reason,
   194→        note=note,
   195→        attestation=attestation,
   196→        review_after=review_after,
   197→        scan_count=scan_count,
   198→    )
   199→    _persist_plan_and_state(
   200→        plan_before=plan_before,
   201→        plan_after=plan,
   202→        plan_path=plan_path,
   203→        state_after=(state_data if resolved else None),
   204→        state_file=state_file,
   205→    )
   206→
   207→    label = {"temporary": "Skipped", "permanent": "Wontfixed", "false_positive": "Marked false positive"}
   208→    print(colorize(f"  {label[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))
   209→    if review_after:
   210→        print(colorize(f"  Will re-surface after {review_after} scan(s).", "dim"))
   211→
   212→
   213→def cmd_plan_unskip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   214→    """Unskip findings — bring back to queue."""
   215→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   216→    state = runtime.state
   217→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   218→        return
   219→    plan_path = _plan_path_for_runtime(runtime.state_path)
   220→
   221→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   222→
   223→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   224→    plan_before = deepcopy(plan)
   225→    # For unskip we need to match against all statuses (skipped items may be wontfix/fp)
   226→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan, status_filter="all")
   227→    if not finding_ids:
   228→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   229→        return
   230→
   231→    count, need_reopen = unskip_items(plan, finding_ids)
   232→
   233→    # Reopen permanent/false_positive items in state
   234→    reopened: list[str] = []
   235→    state_data: dict | None = None
   236→    state_file: Path | None = None
   237→    if need_reopen:
   238→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   239→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   240→
   241→        state_file = state_path(args)
   242→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   243→        for fid in need_reopen:
   244→            reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "open"))
   245→    _persist_plan_and_state(
   246→        plan_before=plan_before,
   247→        plan_after=plan,
   248→        plan_path=plan_path,
   249→        state_after=(state_data if reopened else None),
   250→        state_file=state_file,
   251→    )
   252→    if need_reopen:
   253→        print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s) in state.", "dim"))
   254→
   255→    print(colorize(f"  Unskipped {count} item(s) — back in queue.", "green"))
   256→
   257→
   258→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   259→# Reopen

   262→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   263→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   264→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
   265→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   266→
   267→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   268→    state_file = state_path(args)
   269→    plan_path = _plan_path_for_runtime(state_file)
   270→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   271→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   272→    plan_before = deepcopy(plan)
   273→
   274→    reopened: list[str] = []
   275→    for pattern in patterns:
   276→        reopened.extend(
   277→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")
   278→        )
   279→
   280→    if not reopened:
   281→        print(colorize("  No resolved findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   282→        return
   283→
   284→    # Remove from skipped if present, and ensure all reopened IDs are in queue
   285→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   286→    count = 0
   287→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   288→    for fid in reopened:
   289→        if fid in skipped:
   290→            skipped.pop(fid)
   291→            count += 1
   292→        if fid not in order:
   293→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   294→            order.add(fid)
   295→            count += 1
   296→    _persist_plan_and_state(
   297→        plan_before=plan_before,
   298→        plan_after=plan,
   299→        plan_path=plan_path,
   300→        state_after=state_data,
   301→        state_file=state_file,
   302→    )
   303→
   304→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s).", "green"))
   305→    if count:
   306→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   307→
   308→
   309→def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   310→    """Mark findings as fixed — delegates to cmd_resolve for rich UX."""
   311→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
   312→        show_attestation_requirement,
   313→        validate_attestation,
   314→    )
   315→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
   316→
   317→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   318→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   319→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   320→
   321→    # Pre-validate attestation before delegating (avoids stale hint in resolve)
   322→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   323→        show_attestation_requirement("Plan done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   324→        return
   325→
   326→    # Build a Namespace that cmd_resolve expects
   327→    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
   328→        status="fixed",
   329→        patterns=patterns,
   330→        note=note,
   331→        attest=attestation,
   332→        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
   333→        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
   334→        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
   335→        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
   336→        exclude=getattr(args, "exclude", None),
   337→    )
   338→
   339→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve
   340→
   341→    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
   342→
   343→
   344→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   345→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   346→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   347→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   348→
   349→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   350→    plan_path = _plan_path_for_runtime(runtime.state_path)
   351→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   352→    if clear_flag:
   353→        clear_focus(plan)
   354→        save_plan(plan, plan_path)
   355→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   356→        return
   357→
   358→    if not cluster_name:
   359→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   360→        if active:
   361→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   362→        else:
   363→            print("  No active focus.")
   364→        return
   365→
   366→    try:
   367→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   368→    except ValueError as ex:
   369→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   370→        return
   371→    save_plan(plan, plan_path)
   372→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   373→
   374→
   375→__all__ = [

1→"""Aggregate mechanical detector findings into structured evidence clusters.
     2→
     3→Reads ALL state findings and produces signal clusters organized for holistic
     4→review context enrichment.  The LLM reviewer gets hotspots and patterns —
     5→not raw finding dumps — so it can investigate root causes.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→
    14→def _normalize_allowed_files(
    15→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None,
    16→) -> set[str] | None:
    17→    """Normalize optional allowed-file scope to slash-normalized relative paths."""
    18→    if allowed_files is None:
    19→        return None
    20→    out: set[str] = set()
    21→    for raw in allowed_files:
    22→        if not isinstance(raw, str):
    23→            continue
    24→        file_path = raw.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    25→        if file_path:
    26→            out.add(file_path)
    27→    return out
    28→
    29→
    30→def gather_mechanical_evidence(
    31→    state: dict[str, Any],
    32→    *,
    33→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    34→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    35→    """Aggregate open findings into evidence clusters for holistic review.
    36→
    37→    Returns a dict of named clusters.  Empty dict when no findings exist.
    38→    """
    39→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    40→    if not findings:
    41→        return {}
    42→    allowed_scope = _normalize_allowed_files(allowed_files)
    43→
    44→    # ── Single-pass bucketing ────────────────────────────────────────
    45→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    46→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    47→    smell_counter: Counter[str] = Counter()
    48→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)  # smell_id -> [files]
    49→
    50→    for finding in findings.values():
    51→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
    52→            continue
    53→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    54→            continue
    55→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
    56→        normalized_file = (
    57→            filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    58→            if isinstance(filepath, str)
    59→            else ""
    60→        )
    61→        if allowed_scope is not None and normalized_file not in allowed_scope:
    62→            continue
    63→        det = finding.get("detector", "")
    64→        if det:
    65→            by_detector[det].append(finding)
    66→        if normalized_file and normalized_file != ".":
    67→            by_file[normalized_file].append(finding)
    68→        # Track smell subtypes for systemic pattern detection
    69→        if det == "smells":
    70→            detail = finding.get("detail", {})
    71→            smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
    72→            if smell_id:
    73→                smell_counter[smell_id] += 1
    74→                smell_files[smell_id].append(normalized_file)
    75→
    76→    if not by_detector:
    77→        return {}
    78→
    79→    evidence: dict[str, Any] = {}
    80→
    81→    # ── Cluster A: Structural Complexity ─────────────────────────────
    82→    complexity_hotspots = _build_complexity_hotspots(by_detector, by_file)
    83→    if complexity_hotspots:
    84→        evidence["complexity_hotspots"] = complexity_hotspots
    85→
    86→    # ── Cluster B: Error & State Hygiene ─────────────────────────────
    87→    error_hotspots = _build_error_hotspots(by_detector)
    88→    if error_hotspots:
    89→        evidence["error_hotspots"] = error_hotspots
    90→
    91→    mutable_globals = _build_mutable_globals(by_detector)
    92→    if mutable_globals:
    93→        evidence["mutable_globals"] = mutable_globals
    94→
    95→    # ── Cluster C: Dependency Architecture ───────────────────────────
    96→    boundary_violations = _build_boundary_violations(by_detector)
    97→    if boundary_violations:
    98→        evidence["boundary_violations"] = boundary_violations
    99→
   100→    dead_code = _build_dead_code(by_detector)

1→"""File selection and staleness tracking for review."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→import logging
     7→import re
     8→from dataclasses import dataclass
     9→from datetime import UTC, datetime
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import read_file_text, rel
    14→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context import (
    15→    abs_path,
    16→    dep_graph_lookup,
    17→    importer_count,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.languages import get_lang
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→# Files with these name patterns have low subjective review value —
    25→# they're mostly declarations (types, constants, enums) not logic.
    26→LOW_VALUE_NAMES = re.compile(r"(?:^|/)(?:types|constants|enums|index)\.[a-z]+$")
    27→# Minimum LOC to be worth a review slot.
    28→MIN_REVIEW_LOC = 20
    29→
    30→
    31→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    32→class ReviewSelectionOptions:
    33→    """Configuration for review file selection."""
    34→
    35→    max_files: int | None = None
    36→    max_age_days: int = 30
    37→    force_refresh: bool = True
    38→    files: list[str] | None = None
    39→
    40→
    41→def hash_file(filepath: str) -> str:
    42→    """Compute a content hash for a file."""
    43→    try:
    44→        content = Path(filepath).read_bytes()
    45→        return hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest()[:16]
    46→    except OSError:
    47→        return ""
    48→
    49→
    50→def select_files_for_review(
    51→    lang: Any,
    52→    path: Path,
    53→    state: dict,
    54→    options: ReviewSelectionOptions | None = None,
    55→) -> list[str]:
    56→    """Select production files for review, priority-sorted.
    57→
    58→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
    59→    """
    60→    resolved_options = options or ReviewSelectionOptions()
    61→
    62→    files = resolved_options.files
    63→    if files is None:
    64→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    65→
    66→    cache = state.get("review_cache", {}).get("files", {})
    67→    now = datetime.now(UTC)
    68→    candidates = []
    69→
    70→    for filepath in files:
    71→        rpath = rel(filepath)
    72→
    73→        # Skip non-production files
    74→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
    75→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath)
    76→            if zone.value in ("test", "generated", "vendor"):
    77→                continue
    78→
    79→        # Skip if cached, content unchanged, and not stale
    80→        if not resolved_options.force_refresh:
    81→            entry = cache.get(rpath)
    82→            if entry:
    83→                current_hash = hash_file(abs_path(filepath))
    84→                if current_hash and current_hash == entry.get("content_hash"):
    85→                    reviewed_at = entry.get("reviewed_at", "")
    86→                    if reviewed_at:
    87→                        try:
    88→                            reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
    89→                            age_days = (now - reviewed).days
    90→                            if age_days <= resolved_options.max_age_days:
    91→                                continue  # Still fresh
    92→                        except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
    93→                            entry["reviewed_at"] = ""
    94→                            logger.debug(
    95→                                "Invalid reviewed_at value %r for %s: %s",
    96→                                reviewed_at,
    97→                                rpath,
    98→                                exc,
    99→                            )
   100→
   101→        priority = _compute_review_priority(filepath, lang, state)
   102→        if priority >= 0:  # Negative = filtered out (too small)
   103→            candidates.append((filepath, priority))
   104→
   105→    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
   106→    selected = [f for f, _ in candidates]
   107→    if resolved_options.max_files is None:
   108→        return selected
   109→    return selected[: resolved_options.max_files]
   110→
   111→
   112→def _compute_review_priority(filepath: str, lang, state: dict) -> int:
   113→    """Higher = more important to review.
   114→
   115→    Prioritizes implementation files with high blast radius and existing findings.
   116→    Deprioritizes types/constants files (low subjective review value).
   117→    """
   118→    score = 0
   119→    rpath = rel(filepath)
   120→
   121→    content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   122→    loc = len(content.splitlines()) if content is not None else 0
   123→
   124→    # Skip tiny files — not enough to review
   125→    if loc < MIN_REVIEW_LOC:
   126→        return -1
   127→
   128→    # Low-value files: language-provided pattern or generic fallback.
   129→    is_low_value = is_low_value_file(rpath, lang)
   130→
   131→    # High blast radius (many importers)
   132→    if lang.dep_graph:
   133→        entry = dep_graph_lookup(lang.dep_graph, filepath)
   134→        ic = importer_count(entry)
   135→        if is_low_value:
   136→            score += ic * 2
   137→        else:
   138→            score += ic * 10
   139→
   140→    # Already has programmatic findings (compound value — review will be richer)
   141→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   142→    n_findings = sum(
   143→        1 for f in findings.values() if f.get("file") == rpath and f["status"] == "open"
   144→    )
   145→    score += n_findings * 5
   146→
   147→    # High-complexity files with wontfixed structural findings
   148→    # (mechanical detector says "complex" but can't say why — subjective review can)
   149→    n_wontfix_structural = sum(
   150→        1
   151→        for f in findings.values()
   152→        if f.get("file") == rpath
   153→        and f["status"] == "wontfix"
   154→        and f.get("detector") in ("structural", "smells")
   155→    )
   156→    if n_wontfix_structural:
   157→        score += n_wontfix_structural * 15  # Strong boost — these need human insight
   158→
   159→    # Complexity score from mechanical detectors (if available)
   160→    complexity_map = getattr(lang, "complexity_map", None)
   161→    if isinstance(complexity_map, dict) and complexity_map.get(rpath, 0) > 100:
   162→        score += 20  # Very complex files need subjective review most
   163→
   164→    # Larger files have more to review
   165→    score += loc // 50
   166→
   167→    # Low-value penalty — push toward bottom but don't exclude entirely
   168→    if is_low_value:
   169→        score = score // 3
   170→
   171→    return score
   172→
   173→
   174→def low_value_pattern(lang_or_name: Any = None) -> re.Pattern[str]:
   175→    """Return the low-value filename regex for a language, with generic fallback."""
   176→    if lang_or_name is not None and hasattr(lang_or_name, "review_low_value_pattern"):
   177→        pattern = getattr(lang_or_name, "review_low_value_pattern", None)
   178→        if isinstance(pattern, re.Pattern):
   179→            return pattern
   180→
   181→    if isinstance(lang_or_name, str):
   182→        try:
   183→            pattern = getattr(
   184→                get_lang(lang_or_name), "review_low_value_pattern", None
   185→            )
   186→            if isinstance(pattern, re.Pattern):
   187→                return pattern
   188→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
   189→            pattern = None
   190→            logger.debug(
   191→                "Unable to load low-value review pattern for %s: %s", lang_or_name, exc
   192→            )
   193→
   194→    return LOW_VALUE_NAMES
   195→
   196→
   197→def is_low_value_file(filepath: str, lang_or_name=None) -> bool:
   198→    """Whether a file path is low-value for subjective review."""
   199→    pattern = low_value_pattern(lang_or_name)
   200→    return bool(pattern.search(filepath))
```

1→"""File selection and context section builders for holistic review."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import re
     7→from collections import Counter
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel, resolve_path
    11→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES
    12→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    13→    ERROR_PATTERNS as _ERROR_PATTERNS,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    16→    FUNC_NAME_RE as _FUNC_NAME_RE,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    19→    extract_imported_names as _extract_imported_names,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context import file_excerpt, importer_count
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→
    26→def select_holistic_files(path: Path, lang: object, files: list[str] | None) -> list[str]:
    27→    selected = files if files is not None else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
    28→    if not selected:
    29→        return []
    30→
    31→    zone_map = getattr(lang, "zone_map", None)
    32→    if zone_map is None:
    33→        return selected
    34→
    35→    filtered: list[str] = []
    36→    for filepath in selected:
    37→        try:
    38→            zone = zone_map.get(filepath)
    39→            zone_value = getattr(zone, "value", str(zone))
    40→        except (AttributeError, KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
    41→            zone_value = "production"
    42→        if zone_value in EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES:
    43→            continue
    44→        filtered.append(filepath)
    45→    return filtered
    46→
    47→
    48→def _architecture_context(lang, file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
    49→    arch: dict = {}
    50→    if not lang.dep_graph:
    51→        return arch
    52→
    53→    importer_counts = {}
    54→    for filepath, entry in lang.dep_graph.items():
    55→        entry_importer_count = importer_count(entry)
    56→        if entry_importer_count > 0:
    57→            importer_counts[rel(filepath)] = entry_importer_count
    58→    top_imported = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])[:10]
    59→    arch["god_modules"] = [
    60→        {"file": filepath, "importers": count, "excerpt": file_excerpt(filepath) or ""}
    61→        for filepath, count in top_imported
    62→        if count >= 5
    63→    ]
    64→    arch["top_imported"] = dict(top_imported)
    65→    return arch
    66→
    67→
    68→def _coupling_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
    69→    coupling: dict = {}
    70→    module_level_io = []
    71→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
    72→        for idx, raw_line in enumerate(content.splitlines()[:50]):
    73→            stripped = raw_line.strip()
    74→            if stripped.startswith(
    75→                ("def ", "class ", "async def ", "if ", "#", "@", "import ", "from ")
    76→            ):
    77→                continue
    78→            if re.search(
    79→                r"\b(?:open|connect|requests?\.|urllib|subprocess|os\.system)\b",
    80→                stripped,
    81→            ):
    82→                module_level_io.append(
    83→                    {
    84→                        "file": rel(filepath),
    85→                        "line": idx + 1,
    86→                        "code": stripped[:100],
    87→                    }
    88→                )
    89→    if module_level_io:
    90→        coupling["module_level_io"] = module_level_io[:20]
    91→    return coupling
    92→
    93→
    94→def _naming_conventions_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
    95→    dir_styles: dict[str, Counter] = {}
    96→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
    97→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
    98→        if len(parts) < 2:
    99→            continue
   100→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   101→        counter = dir_styles.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   102→        for name in _FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   103→            if "_" in name and name.islower():
   104→                counter["snake_case"] += 1
   105→            elif name[0].islower() and any(ch.isupper() for ch in name):
   106→                counter["camelCase"] += 1
   107→            elif name[0].isupper():
   108→                counter["PascalCase"] += 1
   109→    return {
   110→        name: dict(counter.most_common(3))
   111→        for name, counter in dir_styles.items()
   112→        if sum(counter.values()) >= 3
   113→    }
   114→
   115→
   116→def _sibling_behavior_context(
   117→    file_contents: dict[str, str], *, base_path: Path | str | None = None
   118→) -> dict:
   119→    root = Path(base_path).resolve() if base_path is not None else None
   120→
   121→    def _bucket_for(filepath: str) -> str | None:
   122→        target = Path(filepath).resolve()
   123→        if root is not None:
   124→            try:
   125→                parts = target.relative_to(root).parts
   126→                if len(parts) >= 2:
   127→                    return f"{parts[0]}/"
   128→                return None
   129→            except ValueError:
   130→                logger.debug("Path %s not relative to root %s, using fallback bucket", filepath, root)
   131→                parts = target.parts
   132→                if len(parts) < 2:
   133→                    return None
   134→                return f"{parts[-2]}/"
   135→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   136→        if len(parts) < 2:
   137→            return None
   138→        return f"{parts[-2]}/"
   139→
   140→    def _display_path(filepath: str) -> str:
   141→        target = Path(filepath).resolve()
   142→        if root is not None:
   143→            try:
   144→                return target.relative_to(root).as_posix()
   145→            except ValueError:
   146→                logger.debug("Path %s not relative to root %s, using rel() fallback", filepath, root)
   147→                fallback_path = rel(filepath)
   148→                return fallback_path
   149→        return rel(filepath)
   150→
   151→    dir_imports: dict[str, dict[str, set[str]]] = {}
   152→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   153→        dir_name = _bucket_for(filepath)
   154→        if dir_name is None:
   155→            continue
   156→        file_rel = _display_path(filepath)
   157→        dir_imports.setdefault(dir_name, {})[file_rel] = _extract_imported_names(content)
   158→
   159→    sibling_behavior: dict = {}
   160→    for dir_name, file_names_map in dir_imports.items():
   161→        total = len(file_names_map)
   162→        if total < 3:
   163→            continue
   164→        name_counts: Counter = Counter()
   165→        for names in file_names_map.values():
   166→            for name in names:
   167→                name_counts[name] += 1
   168→        threshold = total * 0.6
   169→        shared = {
   170→            name: count for name, count in name_counts.items() if count >= threshold
   171→        }
   172→        if not shared:
   173→            continue
   174→        outliers = []
   175→        for file_rel, names in file_names_map.items():
   176→            missing = [name for name in shared if name not in names]
   177→            if missing:
   178→                outliers.append({"file": file_rel, "missing": sorted(missing)})
   179→        if not outliers:
   180→            continue
   181→        sibling_behavior[dir_name] = {
   182→            "shared_patterns": {
   183→                name: {"count": count, "total": total}
   184→                for name, count in sorted(shared.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])
   185→            },
   186→            "outliers": sorted(
   187→                outliers, key=lambda item: len(item["missing"]), reverse=True
   188→            ),
   189→        }
   190→    return sibling_behavior
   191→
   192→
   193→def _error_strategy_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
   194→    dir_errors: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   195→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   196→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   197→        if len(parts) < 2:
   198→            continue
   199→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   200→        counter = dir_errors.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   201→        for pattern_name, pattern in _ERROR_PATTERNS.items():
   202→            matches = pattern.findall(content)
   203→            if matches:
   204→                counter[pattern_name] += len(matches)
   205→    return {
   206→        name: dict(counter.most_common(5))
   207→        for name, counter in dir_errors.items()
   208→        if sum(counter.values()) >= 2
   209→    }
   210→
   211→
   212→def _in_allowed_files(filepath: object, allowed_files: set[str] | None) -> bool:
   213→    if allowed_files is None:
   214→        return True
   215→    return isinstance(filepath, str) and filepath in allowed_files
   216→
   217→
   218→def _dependencies_context(
   219→    state: dict,
   220→    *,
   221→    allowed_files: set[str] | None = None,
   222→) -> dict:
   223→    cycle_findings = []
   224→    cycle_files: list[str] = []
   225→    cycle_edges: list[dict[str, object]] = []
   226→    seen_cycle_files: set[str] = set()
   227→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   228→    if not isinstance(findings, dict):
   229→        findings = {}
   230→    for finding in findings.values():
   231→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
   232→            continue
   233→        if finding.get("detector") != "cycles" or finding.get("status") != "open":
   234→            continue
   235→        if not _in_allowed_files(finding.get("file", ""), allowed_files):
   236→            continue
   237→        cycle_findings.append(finding)
   238→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   239→        if isinstance(detail, dict):
   240→            raw_cycle_paths = detail.get("files", [])
   241→            normalized_paths: list[str] = []
   242→            if isinstance(raw_cycle_paths, list):
   243→                for raw_path in raw_cycle_paths:
   244→                    if not isinstance(raw_path, str):
   245→                        continue
   246→                    path = raw_path.strip()
   247→                    if not path:
   248→                        continue
   249→                    normalized_paths.append(path)
   250→                    if path in seen_cycle_files:
   251→                        continue
   252→                    seen_cycle_files.add(path)
   253→                    cycle_files.append(path)
   254→            if normalized_paths:
   255→                raw_length = detail.get("length")
   256→                length = (
   257→                    int(raw_length)
   258→                    if isinstance(raw_length, int | float)
   259→                    else len(normalized_paths)
   260→                )
   261→                cycle_edges.append({"files": normalized_paths, "length": max(1, length)})
   262→    if not cycle_findings:
   263→        return {}
   264→    return {
   265→        "existing_cycles": len(cycle_findings),
   266→        "cycle_summaries": [
   267→            str(finding.get("summary", "")).strip()[:120]
   268→            for finding in cycle_findings[:10]
   269→            if str(finding.get("summary", "")).strip()
   270→        ],
   271→        "cycle_files": cycle_files[:40],
   272→        "cycle_edges": cycle_edges[:10],
   273→    }
   274→
   275→
   276→def _testing_context(
   277→    lang,
   278→    state: dict,
   279→    file_contents: dict[str, str],
   280→    *,
   281→    allowed_files: set[str] | None = None,
   282→) -> dict:
   283→    testing: dict = {"total_files": len(file_contents)}
   284→    if not lang.dep_graph:
   285→        return testing
   286→
   287→    tc_findings = {
   288→        finding["file"]
   289→        for finding in state.get("findings", {}).values()
   290→        if finding.get("detector") == "test_coverage"
   291→        and finding.get("status") == "open"
   292→        and _in_allowed_files(finding.get("file", ""), allowed_files)
   293→    }
   294→    if not tc_findings:
   295→        return testing
   296→
   297→    critical_untested = []
   298→    for filepath in tc_findings:
   299→        entry = lang.dep_graph.get(resolve_path(filepath), {})
   300→        entry_importer_count = importer_count(entry)
   301→        if entry_importer_count >= 3:
   302→            critical_untested.append(
   303→                {"file": filepath, "importers": entry_importer_count}
   304→            )
   305→    testing["critical_untested"] = sorted(
   306→        critical_untested,
   307→        key=lambda item: -item["importers"],
   308→    )[:10]
   309→    return testing
   310→
   311→
   312→def _api_surface_context(lang, file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
   313→    api_surface_fn = getattr(lang, "review_api_surface_fn", None)
   314→    if not callable(api_surface_fn):
   315→        return {}
   316→    computed = api_surface_fn(file_contents)
   317→    return computed if isinstance(computed, dict) else {}
   318→

1→"""Helpers used by holistic review preparation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
     8→
     9→HOLISTIC_WORKFLOW = [
    10→    "Read .desloppify/query.json for context, excerpts, and investigation batches",
    11→    "For each batch: start from listed seed files, then explore likely hotspots/unreviewed neighbors; evaluate the batch's dimensions (batches are independent — parallelize)",
    12→    "Cross-reference findings with the sibling_behavior and convention data",
    13→    "IMPORTANT: findings must be defects only — never positive observations. High scores capture quality; findings capture problems.",
    14→    "Write ALL findings to findings.json — do NOT fix code before importing. Import creates tracked state entries that let desloppify correlate fixes to findings.",
    15→    "Preferred local Codex path: desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import",
    16→    "Claude cloud durable path: run `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`, follow the session template/instructions, then run the printed `--external-submit` command",
    17→    "Fallback path: `desloppify review --import findings.json` (findings only). Use manual override only for emergency/provisional imports.",
    18→    "AFTER importing: run `desloppify show review --status open` to see the work queue, then fix each finding in code and `desloppify plan done <id>`",
    19→]
    20→
    21→
    22→def append_full_sweep_batch(
    23→    *,
    24→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
    25→    dims: list[str],
    26→    all_files: list[str],
    27→    lang: Any,
    28→    max_files: int | None = None,
    29→) -> None:
    30→    """Append an optional cross-cutting full-codebase batch."""
    31→    if not dims:
    32→        return
    33→    all_rel_files: list[str] = []
    34→    for filepath in all_files:
    35→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
    36→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath)
    37→            if zone.value in ("test", "generated", "vendor"):
    38→                continue
    39→        all_rel_files.append(rel(filepath))
    40→        if isinstance(max_files, int) and max_files > 0 and len(all_rel_files) >= max_files:
    41→            break
    42→    if not all_rel_files:
    43→        return
    44→    batches.append(
    45→        {
    46→            "name": "Full Codebase Sweep",
    47→            "dimensions": list(dims),
    48→            "files_to_read": all_rel_files,
    49→            "why": "thorough default: evaluate cross-cutting quality across all production files",
    50→        }
    51→    )
    52→

1→"""Plan persistence — load/save with atomic writes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import Callable
    11→
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    13→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    14→    PLAN_VERSION,
    15→    PlanModel,
    16→    empty_plan,
    17→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    18→    validate_plan,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import get_state_dir, json_default
    21→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→
    26→class _DynamicPathProxy:
    27→    """Path-like proxy that resolves against runtime-root-aware accessors."""
    28→
    29→    def __init__(self, resolver: Callable[[], Path], *, label: str) -> None:
    30→        self._resolver = resolver
    31→        self._label = label
    32→
    33→    def _value(self) -> Path:
    34→        return self._resolver()
    35→
    36→    def __fspath__(self) -> str:
    37→        return str(self._value())
    38→
    39→    def __str__(self) -> str:
    40→        return str(self._value())
    41→
    42→    def __repr__(self) -> str:
    43→        return f"{self._label}({self._value()!s})"
    44→
    45→    def __truediv__(self, other: object) -> Path:
    46→        return self._value() / other
    47→
    48→    def __rtruediv__(self, other: object) -> Path:
    49→        return Path(other) / self._value()
    50→
    51→    def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> object:
    52→        return getattr(self._value(), name)
    53→
    54→
    55→def get_plan_file() -> Path:
    56→    """Return default plan file anchored at the active runtime state dir."""
    57→    return get_state_dir() / "plan.json"
    58→
    59→
    60→PLAN_FILE = _DynamicPathProxy(get_plan_file, label="PLAN_FILE")
    61→
    62→
    63→def _log_backup_failure(exc: OSError) -> None:
    64→    """Record plan-backup copy failures before atomic write."""
    65→    logger.debug("Failed to create plan backup: %s", exc)
    66→
    67→
    68→def load_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> PlanModel:
    69→    """Load plan from disk, or return empty plan on missing/corruption."""
    70→    plan_path = path or get_plan_file()
    71→    if not plan_path.exists():
    72→        return empty_plan()
    73→
    74→    try:
    75→        data = json.loads(plan_path.read_text())
    76→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
    77→        print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→        return empty_plan()
    79→
    80→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    81→        print("  Warning: Plan file root must be a JSON object. Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    82→        return empty_plan()
    83→
    84→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    85→    if version > PLAN_VERSION:
    86→        print(
    87→            f"  Warning: Plan file version {version} is newer than supported "
    88→            f"({PLAN_VERSION}). Some features may not work correctly.",
    89→            file=sys.stderr,
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    ensure_plan_defaults(data)
    93→    try:
    94→        validate_plan(data)
    95→    except ValueError as ex:
    96→        print(f"  Warning: Plan invariants invalid ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    97→        return empty_plan()
    98→
    99→    return data  # type: ignore[return-value]
   100→
   101→
   102→def save_plan(plan: PlanModel | dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
   103→    """Validate and save plan to disk atomically."""
   104→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   105→    plan["updated"] = utc_now()
   106→    validate_plan(plan)
   107→
   108→    plan_path = path or get_plan_file()
   109→    plan_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   110→
   111→    content = json.dumps(plan, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
   112→
   113→    if plan_path.exists():
   114→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   115→        try:
   116→            shutil.copy2(str(plan_path), str(backup))
   117→        except OSError as backup_ex:
   118→            _log_backup_failure(backup_ex)
   119→
   120→    try:
   121→        safe_write_text(plan_path, content)
   122→    except OSError as ex:
   123→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save plan: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   124→        raise
   125→
   126→
   127→def plan_path_for_state(state_path: Path) -> Path:
   128→    """Derive plan.json path from a state file path."""
   129→    return state_path.parent / "plan.json"
   130→
   131→
   132→def has_living_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> bool:
   133→    """Return True if a plan.json exists and has user intent."""
   134→    plan_path = path or get_plan_file()
   135→    if not plan_path.exists():
   136→        return False
   137→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   138→    return bool(
   139→        plan.get("queue_order")
   140→        or plan.get("overrides")
   141→        or plan.get("clusters")
   142→    )
   143→
   144→
   145→__all__ = [
   146→    "PLAN_FILE",
   147→    "get_plan_file",
   148→    "has_living_plan",
   149→    "load_plan",
   150→    "plan_path_for_state",
   151→    "save_plan",
   152→]
   153→
