```
   125→
   126→def _collect_reviewed_files_from_batches(
   127→    *,
   128→    batches: list[dict[str, object]],
   129→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   130→) -> list[str]:
   131→    """Collect normalized file paths reviewed in the selected batch set."""
   132→    reviewed: list[str] = []
   133→    seen: set[str] = set()
   134→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   135→        if idx < 0 or idx >= len(batches):
   136→            continue
   137→        batch = batches[idx]
   138→        files = batch.get("files_to_read", [])
   139→        if not isinstance(files, list):
   140→            continue
   141→        for raw in files:
   142→            if not isinstance(raw, str):
   143→                continue
   144→            path = raw.strip().strip(",'\"")
   145→            if not path or path in {".", ".."}:
   146→                continue
   147→            if path.endswith("/"):
   148→                continue
   149→            if path in seen:
   150→                continue
   151→            seen.add(path)
   152→            reviewed.append(path)
   153→    return reviewed
   154→
   155→
   156→def _normalize_dimension_list(raw: object) -> list[str]:
   157→    """Normalize dimension collections to a stable, de-duplicated list."""
   158→    if not isinstance(raw, list):
   159→        return []
   160→    out: list[str] = []
   161→    seen: set[str] = set()
   162→    for item in raw:
   163→        if not isinstance(item, str):
   164→            continue
   165→        dim = item.strip()
   166→        if not dim or dim in seen:
   167→            continue
   168→        seen.add(dim)
   169→        out.append(dim)
   170→    return out
   171→
   172→
   173→def _scored_dimensions_for_lang(
   174→    lang_name: str,
   175→    *,
   176→    warning_sink: list[NonFatalIssue] | None = None,
   177→) -> list[str]:
   178→    """Return default scored subjective dimensions for one language."""
   179→    try:
   180→        default_dims, _, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
   181→    except (ValueError, RuntimeError) as exc:
   182→        report_non_fatal_issue(
   183→            NonFatalIssue(
   184→                operation="review.batches.load_dimensions_for_lang",
   185→                target=lang_name,
   186→                error_type=type(exc).__name__,
   187→                message=str(exc),
   188→            ),
   189→            sink=warning_sink,
   190→        )
   191→        return []
   192→    return _normalize_dimension_list(default_dims)
   193→
   194→
   195→def _missing_scored_dimensions(
   196→    *,
   197→    selected_dims: list[str],
   198→    scored_dims: list[str],
   199→) -> list[str]:
   200→    selected = set(selected_dims)
   201→    return [dim for dim in scored_dims if dim not in selected]
   202→
   203→
   204→def _missing_dimensions_command(*, missing_dims: list[str], scan_path: str) -> str:
   205→    """Return rerun command for missing subjective dimensions."""
   206→    base = "desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import"
   207→    if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
   208→        base += f" --path {shlex.quote(scan_path)}"
   209→    if missing_dims:
   210→        base += f" --dimensions {','.join(missing_dims)}"
   211→    return base
   212→
   213→
   214→def _print_preflight_dimension_scope_notice(
   215→    *,
   216→    selected_dims: list[str],
   217→    scored_dims: list[str],
   218→    explicit_selection: bool,
   219→    scan_path: str,
   220→    colorize_fn,
   221→) -> None:
   222→    """Print trigger-time notice when run scope is a scored-dimension subset."""
   223→    if not scored_dims:
   224→        return
   225→    missing_dims = _missing_scored_dimensions(
   226→        selected_dims=selected_dims,
   227→        scored_dims=scored_dims,
   228→    )
   229→    if not missing_dims:
   230→        return
   231→
   232→    covered_count = len([dim for dim in selected_dims if dim in set(scored_dims)])
   233→    scope_reason = (
   234→        "explicit --dimensions selection"
   235→        if explicit_selection
   236→        else "language default review dimension set"
   237→    )
   238→    tone = "yellow" if explicit_selection else "red"
   239→    print(
   240→        colorize_fn(
   241→            "  WARNING: this run targets "
   242→            f"{covered_count}/{len(scored_dims)} scored subjective dimensions "
   243→            f"({scope_reason}).",
   244→            tone,
   245→        )
   246→    )
   247→    preview = ", ".join(missing_dims[:5])
   248→    if len(missing_dims) > 5:
   249→        preview = f"{preview}, +{len(missing_dims) - 5} more"
   250→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Missing from this run: {preview}", "yellow"))
   251→    print(
   252→        colorize_fn(
   253→            "  Rerun missing dimensions: "
   254→            f"`{_missing_dimensions_command(missing_dims=missing_dims, scan_path=scan_path)}`",
   255→            "dim",
   256→        )
   257→    )
   258→
   259→
   260→def _print_import_dimension_coverage_notice(
   261→    *,
   262→    assessed_dims: list[str],
   263→    scored_dims: list[str],
   264→    scan_path: str,
   265→    colorize_fn,

   268→    if not scored_dims:

   270→    missing_dims = _missing_scored_dimensions(

   274→    if not missing_dims:

   276→

   278→    print(

   297→
   298→
   299→def _run_batch_task(
   300→    *,
   301→    batch_index: int,
   302→    prompt_path: Path,
   303→    output_path: Path,
   304→    log_path: Path,
   305→    repo_root: Path,
   306→    run_codex_batch_fn,
   307→) -> int:
   308→    """Execute one batch prompt and return the runner exit code."""
   309→    try:
   310→        prompt = prompt_path.read_text()
   311→    except OSError as exc:
   312→        raise RuntimeError(
   313→            f"unable to read prompt for batch #{batch_index + 1}: {prompt_path}"
   314→        ) from exc
   315→    return run_codex_batch_fn(
   316→        prompt=prompt,
   317→        repo_root=repo_root,
   318→        output_file=output_path,
   319→        log_file=log_path,
   320→    )
   321→
   322→
   323→def _build_batch_tasks(
   324→    *,
   325→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   326→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
   327→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   328→    log_files: dict[int, Path],
   329→    repo_root: Path,
   330→    run_codex_batch_fn,
   331→) -> dict[int, object]:
   332→    """Build execution tasks keyed by batch index."""
   333→    tasks: dict[int, object] = {}
   334→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   335→        tasks[idx] = partial(
   336→            _run_batch_task,
   337→            batch_index=idx,
   338→            prompt_path=prompt_files[idx],
   339→            output_path=output_files[idx],
   340→            log_path=log_files[idx],
   341→            repo_root=repo_root,
   342→            run_codex_batch_fn=run_codex_batch_fn,
   343→        )
   344→    return tasks
   345→
   346→
   347→@dataclass
   348→class _BatchExecutionTracker:
   349→    """Track batch execution progress, logs, and run-summary output."""
   350→
   351→    stamp: str
   352→    runner: str
   353→    run_parallel: bool
   354→    total_batches: int
   355→    selected_indexes: list[int]
   356→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path]
   357→    output_files: dict[int, Path]
   358→    log_files: dict[int, Path]
   359→    run_dir: Path
   360→    logs_dir: Path
   361→    run_log_path: Path
   362→    immutable_packet_path: Path
   363→    prompt_packet_path: Path
   364→    allow_partial: bool
   365→    max_parallel_batches: int
   366→    batch_timeout_seconds: int
   367→    batch_max_retries: int
   368→    batch_retry_backoff_seconds: float
   369→    heartbeat_seconds: float
   370→    stall_warning_seconds: int
   371→    stall_kill_seconds: int
   372→    safe_write_text_fn: object
   373→    colorize_fn: object
   374→    batch_positions: dict[int, int]
   375→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]]
   376→    summary_created_at: str
   377→    stall_warned_batches: set[int] = field(default_factory=set)
   378→
   379→    def append_run_log(self, message: str) -> None:
   380→        line = f"{datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds')} {message}\n"
   381→        try:
   382→            with self.run_log_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
   383→                handle.write(line)
   384→        except OSError:
   385→            return
   386→
   387→    def report_progress(
   388→        self,
   389→        batch_index: int,
   390→        event: str,
   391→        code: int | None = None,
   392→        **details,
   393→    ) -> None:
   394→        if event == "heartbeat":
   395→            active = details.get("active_batches")
   396→            queued = details.get("queued_batches", [])
   397→            elapsed = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
   398→            if not isinstance(active, list):
   399→                active = []
   400→            if not isinstance(queued, list):
   401→                queued = []
   402→            if not active and not queued:
   403→                return
   404→            segments: list[str] = []
   405→            for idx in active[:6]:
   406→                secs = 0
   407→                if isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   408→                    raw_secs = elapsed.get(idx, 0)
   409→                    secs = int(raw_secs) if isinstance(raw_secs, int | float) else 0
   410→                segments.append(f"#{idx + 1}:{secs}s")
   411→            if len(active) > 6:
   412→                segments.append(f"+{len(active) - 6} more")
   413→            queued_segment = ""
   414→            if queued:
   415→                queued_segment = f", queued {len(queued)}"
   416→            print(
   417→                self.colorize_fn(
   418→                    "  Batch heartbeat: "
   419→                    f"{len(active)}/{self.total_batches} active{queued_segment} "
   420→                    f"({', '.join(segments) if segments else 'running batches pending'})",
   421→                    "dim",
   422→                )
   423→            )
   424→            self.append_run_log(
   425→                "heartbeat "
   426→                f"active={[idx + 1 for idx in active]} queued={[idx + 1 for idx in queued]} "
   427→                f"elapsed={{{', '.join(f'{idx + 1}:{elapsed.get(idx, 0)}' for idx in active)}}}"
   428→            )
   429→            if self.stall_warning_seconds > 0 and isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   430→                slow_active = [
   431→                    idx
   432→                    for idx in active
   433→                    if isinstance(elapsed.get(idx), int | float)
   434→                    and int(elapsed.get(idx) or 0) >= self.stall_warning_seconds
   435→                ]
   436→                newly_warned = [idx for idx in slow_active if idx not in self.stall_warned_batches]
   437→                if newly_warned:
   438→                    self.stall_warned_batches.update(newly_warned)
   439→                    warning_message = (
   440→                        "  Stall warning: batches "
   441→                        f"{[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]} exceeded "
   442→                        f"{self.stall_warning_seconds}s elapsed. "
   443→                        "This may be normal for long runs; review run.log and batch logs."
   444→                    )
   445→                    print(self.colorize_fn(warning_message, "yellow"))
   446→                    self.append_run_log(
   447→                        "stall-warning "
   448→                        f"threshold={self.stall_warning_seconds}s "
   449→                        f"batches={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]}"
   450→                    )
   451→            return
   452→
   453→        position = self.batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
   454→        key = str(batch_index + 1)
   455→        state = self.batch_status.setdefault(
   456→            key,
   457→            {
   458→                "position": position,
   459→                "status": "pending",
   460→                "prompt_path": str(self.prompt_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   461→                "result_path": str(self.output_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   462→                "log_path": str(self.log_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   463→            },
   464→        )
   465→        if event == "queued":
   466→            state["status"] = "queued"
   467→            print(
   468→                self.colorize_fn(
   469→                    f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} queued (#{batch_index + 1})",
   470→                    "dim",
   471→                )
   472→            )
   473→            self.append_run_log(
   474→                f"batch-queued batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches}"
   475→            )
   476→            return
   477→        if event == "start":
   478→            state["status"] = "running"
   479→            state["started_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   480→            print(
   481→                self.colorize_fn(
   482→                    f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} started (#{batch_index + 1})",
   483→                    "dim",
   484→                )
   485→            )
   486→            self.append_run_log(
   487→                f"batch-start batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches}"
   488→            )
   489→            return
   490→        if event == "done":
   491→            status = "done" if code == 0 else f"failed ({code})"
   492→            tone = "dim" if code == 0 else "yellow"
   493→            elapsed_seconds = details.get("elapsed_seconds")
   494→            elapsed_suffix = ""
   495→            if isinstance(elapsed_seconds, int | float):
   496→                elapsed_suffix = f" in {int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))}s"
   497→                state["elapsed_seconds"] = int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))
   498→            state["status"] = "succeeded" if code == 0 else "failed"
   499→            state["exit_code"] = int(code) if isinstance(code, int) else code
   500→            state["completed_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   501→            if batch_index in self.stall_warned_batches:
   502→                self.stall_warned_batches.discard(batch_index)
   503→            print(
   504→                self.colorize_fn(
   505→                    f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} {status}{elapsed_suffix} (#{batch_index + 1})",
   506→                    tone,
   507→                )
   508→            )
   509→            self.append_run_log(
   510→                f"batch-done batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches} "
   511→                f"code={code} elapsed={state.get('elapsed_seconds', 0)}"
   512→            )
   513→
   514→    def record_execution_issue(self, batch_index: int, exc: Exception) -> None:
   515→        if batch_index < 0:
   516→            self.append_run_log(f"execution-error heartbeat error={exc}")
   517→            return
   518→        self.append_run_log(f"execution-error batch={batch_index + 1} error={exc}")
   519→
   520→    def write_run_summary(
   521→        self,
   522→        *,
   523→        successful_batches: list[int],
   524→        failed_batches: list[int],
   525→        interrupted: bool = False,
   526→        interruption_reason: str | None = None,
   527→    ) -> None:
   528→        run_summary: dict[str, object] = {
   529→            "created_at": self.summary_created_at,
   530→            "run_stamp": self.stamp,
   531→            "runner": self.runner,
   532→            "parallel": self.run_parallel,
   533→            "selected_batches": [idx + 1 for idx in self.selected_indexes],
   534→            "successful_batches": successful_batches,
   535→            "failed_batches": failed_batches,
   536→            "allow_partial": self.allow_partial,
   537→            "max_parallel_batches": self.max_parallel_batches if self.run_parallel else 1,
   538→            "batch_timeout_seconds": self.batch_timeout_seconds,
   539→            "batch_max_retries": self.batch_max_retries,
   540→            "batch_retry_backoff_seconds": self.batch_retry_backoff_seconds,
   541→            "batch_heartbeat_seconds": self.heartbeat_seconds if self.run_parallel else None,
   542→            "batch_stall_warning_seconds": self.stall_warning_seconds if self.run_parallel else None,
   543→            "batch_stall_kill_seconds": self.stall_kill_seconds,
   544→            "immutable_packet": str(self.immutable_packet_path),
   545→            "blind_packet": str(self.prompt_packet_path),
   546→            "run_dir": str(self.run_dir),
   547→            "logs_dir": str(self.logs_dir),
   548→            "run_log": str(self.run_log_path),
   549→            "batches": self.batch_status,
   550→        }
   551→        if interrupted:
   552→            run_summary["interrupted"] = True
   553→            if interruption_reason:
   554→                run_summary["interruption_reason"] = interruption_reason
   555→        run_summary_path = self.run_dir / "run_summary.json"
   556→        self.safe_write_text_fn(run_summary_path, json.dumps(run_summary, indent=2) + "\n")
   557→        print(self.colorize_fn(f"  Run summary: {run_summary_path}", "dim"))
   558→        self.append_run_log(f"run-summary {run_summary_path}")
   559→
```

```
491→def _reconcile_plan_best_effort(runtime: ScanRuntime) -> None:
   492→    """Reconcile plan state after scan with typed warning/error handling."""
   493→    from desloppify.engine.plan import (
   494→        auto_cluster_findings,
   495→        load_plan,
   496→        reconcile_plan_after_scan,
   497→        save_plan,
   498→        sync_stale_dimensions,
   499→        sync_unscored_dimensions,
   500→    )
   501→
   502→    plan_path = runtime.state_path.parent / "plan.json" if runtime.state_path else None
   503→    try:
   504→        plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   505→        dirty = False
   506→
   507→        # Standard reconciliation (only if plan has user content)
   508→        if (
   509→            plan.get("queue_order")
   510→            or plan.get("overrides")
   511→            or plan.get("clusters")
   512→            or plan.get("skipped")
   513→        ):
   514→            recon = reconcile_plan_after_scan(plan, runtime.state)
   515→            if recon.changes:
   516→                dirty = True
   517→            if recon.resurfaced:
   518→                print(colorize(
   519→                    f"  Plan: {len(recon.resurfaced)} skipped item(s) re-surfaced after review period.",
   520→                    "cyan",
   521→                ))
   522→
   523→        # Unscored dimension sync (prepend to front, unconditional)
   524→        unscored_sync = sync_unscored_dimensions(plan, runtime.state)
   525→        if unscored_sync.changes:
   526→            dirty = True
   527→        if unscored_sync.injected:
   528→            print(colorize(
   529→                f"  Plan: {len(unscored_sync.injected)} unscored subjective dimension(s) queued for initial review.",
   530→                "cyan",
   531→            ))
   532→
   533→        # Stale dimension sync (always — handles both cleanup and injection)
   534→        dim_sync = sync_stale_dimensions(plan, runtime.state)
   535→        if dim_sync.changes:
   536→            dirty = True
   537→        if dim_sync.pruned:
   538→            print(colorize(
   539→                f"  Plan: {len(dim_sync.pruned)} refreshed subjective dimension(s) removed from queue.",
   540→                "cyan",
   541→            ))
   542→        if dim_sync.injected:
   543→            print(colorize(
   544→                f"  Plan: {len(dim_sync.injected)} stale subjective dimension(s) queued for refresh.",
   545→                "cyan",
   546→            ))
   547→
   548→        # Auto-cluster findings into task groups
   549→        ac_changes = auto_cluster_findings(plan, runtime.state)
   550→        if ac_changes:
   551→            dirty = True
   552→
   553→        if dirty:
   554→            save_plan(plan, plan_path)
   555→    except _PLAN_SYNC_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
   556→        runtime.state["plan_sync_failed"] = True
   557→        logger.warning("Plan reconciliation skipped due to runtime error: %s", exc)
   558→        _record_scan_warning(
   559→            runtime,
   560→            code="plan_sync_failed",
   561→            message=f"plan reconciliation skipped ({exc})",
   562→        )
   563→    except _PLAN_SYNC_UNEXPECTED_EXCEPTIONS as exc:

   565→        logger.warning(

   567→            exc,
   568→            exc_info=True,
   569→        )

   572→            code="plan_sync_failed_unexpected",
   573→            message=f"plan reconciliation skipped due to unexpected error ({exc})",
   574→        )
```

## Report: desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py Refactoring Status

Based on my analysis of the current code state:

### 1. **Main Function Line Count**
The file has **1,231 total lines**, but the mega-function has been **successfully refactored away**. The previously problematic 573-line monolith has been broken into **multiple focused functions**:
- `_execute_batch_run()` - 212 lines (lines 990-1201)
- `_run_batch_execution_stage()` - 141 lines (lines 666-806)
- `_merge_and_import_stage()` - 112 lines (lines 809-920)
- `_post_import_scan_stage()` - 67 lines (lines 923-989)
- Plus 12 additional helper functions

### 2. **Injected Function Parameters**
The design has moved from 18 injected function parameters to a **clean dependency injection pattern using dataclasses**:

- **`BatchRunDependencies`** (lines 561-574) - frozen dataclass with 7 function collaborators:
  - `load_or_prepare_packet_fn`
  - `run_codex_batch_fn`
  - `merge_batch_results_fn`
  - `do_import_fn`
  - `run_followup_scan_fn`
  - `safe_write_text_fn`
  - `colorize_fn`

- **`_BatchRunSettings`** (lines 577-590) - execution policy (10 fields)
- **`_BatchExecutionTracker`** (lines 347-558) - stateful progress tracker (dataclass with methods)

The function signatures are now clean and explicit. For example, `_execute_batch_run()` takes just **1 parameter** (`coordinator: ReviewBatchRunCoordinator`) instead of 18+.

### 3. **Nested Closures**
**Reduced from 7 nested closures to 0 at the function level**. Instead:
- Lambda wrappers are **composed at injection time in `batch.py`** (lines 224-275), not nested inside functions
- Each lambda captures its context upfront via `functools.partial` equivalents
- Methods in `_BatchExecutionTracker` dataclass handle stateful behavior that used to be closures

### 4. **Indirection Chain Evolution**
**Old chain (problematic):**
```
cmd_review → _do_run_batches → lambda wrapper → do_run_batches → closure → 
injected function → actual work
```

**New chain (clean):**
```
cmd_review (entrypoint.py:39)
  → _do_run_batches (batch.py:191)
    [builds BatchRunDependencies with lambdas at lines 223-275]
  → review_batches_mod.do_run_batches (batches.py:1204)
    [trivial delegator to coordinator]
  → ReviewBatchRunCoordinator.run() (batches.py:615-616)
    [calls _execute_batch_run]
  → _execute_batch_run (batches.py:990)
    [orchestrates three stages]
    → _run_batch_execution_stage (batches.py:666)
    → _merge_and_import_stage (batches.py:809)
    → _post_import_scan_stage (batches.py:923)
```

### 5. **Architecture Assessment**
**Status: Successfully refactored from mega-function to object-oriented design**

The solution implements:
- ✓ **Dataclass-based dependency injection** (`BatchRunDependencies`, `_BatchRunSettings`)
- ✓ **Stateful tracker object** (`_BatchExecutionTracker`) instead of closure-based state
- ✓ **Coordinator pattern** (`ReviewBatchRunCoordinator`) for single responsibility
- ✓ **Stage-based orchestration** instead of monolithic flow
- ✓ **Clean lambda injection at composition root** (batch.py) instead of nested in functions

**Indirection depth:** Now manageable at 5-6 levels (reasonable for a CLI → orchestrator → stages architecture), down from the original problematic 7+ nested closures.

The refactoring follows clean architecture principles: dependency injection is explicit, testable, and readable.

```
1→"""Concern generators — mechanical findings → subjective review bridge.
     2→
     3→Concerns are ephemeral: computed on-demand from current state, never persisted.
     4→Only LLM-confirmed concerns become persistent Finding objects via review import.
     5→
     6→Generators focus on cross-cutting synthesis — bundling all signals per file so
     7→the LLM gets a complete picture, and surfacing systemic patterns across files
     8→that no single detector captures.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import hashlib
    14→from collections import defaultdict
    15→from dataclasses import dataclass
    16→from typing import Any, Literal, TypedDict, cast
    17→
    18→from desloppify.core.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    22→class Concern:
    23→    """A potential design problem surfaced by mechanical signals."""
    24→
    25→    type: str  # concern classification
    26→    file: str  # primary file (relative path)
    27→    summary: str  # human-readable 1-liner
    28→    evidence: tuple[str, ...]  # supporting data points
    29→    question: str  # specific question for LLM to evaluate
    30→    fingerprint: str  # stable hash for dismissal tracking
    31→    source_findings: tuple[str, ...]  # finding IDs that triggered this
    32→
    33→
    34→class ConcernSignals(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    """Typed signal payload extracted from mechanical findings."""
    36→
    37→    max_params: float
    38→    max_nesting: float
    39→    loc: float
    40→    function_count: float
    41→    monster_loc: float
    42→    monster_funcs: list[str]
    43→
    44→
    45→_NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS = ("max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count")
    46→SignalKey = Literal["max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count", "monster_loc"]
    47→
    48→
    49→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    50→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    51→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    52→        return
    53→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    54→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    55→
    56→
    57→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    58→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    59→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    60→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    61→
    62→
    63→def _is_dismissed(
    64→    dismissals: dict, fingerprint: str, source_finding_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    65→) -> bool:
    66→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source findings unchanged."""
    67→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    68→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    69→        return False
    70→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_finding_ids", []))
    71→    return prev_sources == set(source_finding_ids)
    72→
    73→
    74→def _open_findings(state: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    75→    """Return all open findings from state."""
    76→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    77→    return [
    78→        f for f in findings.values()
    79→        if isinstance(f, dict) and f.get("status") == "open"
    80→    ]
    81→
    82→
    83→def _group_by_file(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    84→    """Group open findings by file, excluding holistic (file='.')."""
    85→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    86→    for f in _open_findings(state):
    87→        file = f.get("file", "")
    88→        if file and file != ".":
    89→            by_file[file].append(f)
    90→    return dict(by_file)
    91→
    92→
    93→# ── Signal extraction ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    94→
    95→
    96→def _extract_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
    97→    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's findings."""
    98→    signals: ConcernSignals = {}
    99→    monster_funcs: list[str] = []
   100→
   101→    for f in findings:
   102→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   103→        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
   104→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
   105→
   106→        if det == "structural":
   107→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   108→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   109→                for key in _NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS:
   110→                    _update_max_signal(signals, cast(SignalKey, key), s.get(key, 0))
   111→
   112→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   113→            _update_max_signal(signals, "monster_loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
   114→            func = detail.get("function", "")
   115→            if isinstance(func, str) and func:
   116→                monster_funcs.append(func)
   117→
   118→    if monster_funcs:
   119→        signals["monster_funcs"] = monster_funcs
   120→    return signals
   121→
   122→
   123→def _has_elevated_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
   124→    """Does any finding have signals strong enough to flag on its own?"""
   125→    for f in findings:
   126→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   127→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
   128→
   129→        if det == "structural":
   130→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   131→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   132→                if s.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   133→                    return True
   134→                if s.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   135→                    return True
   136→                if s.get("loc", 0) >= 300:
   137→                    return True
   138→
   139→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   140→            return True
   141→
   142→        if det in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication", "coupling",
   143→                    "responsibility_cohesion"):
   144→            return True
   145→
   146→    return False
   147→
   148→
   149→# ── Concern classification ───────────────────────────────────────────
   150→
```

```
31→def _persist_plan_and_state(
    32→    *,
    33→    plan_before: dict,

    36→    state_after: dict | None = None,

    38→) -> None:

    43→
    44→    from desloppify import state as state_mod

    58→                file=sys.stderr,
    59→            )
    60→        raise
```

Excellent. Now I have enough information to provide a comprehensive report. Let me compile my findings:

## Summary: File Path Normalization in the Review System

Based on my research, **all three functions you mentioned still exist**, and the problem of multiple normalization strategies **persists, though with some consolidation**. Here's what I found:

### 1. **`_collect_allowed_review_files` (still exists)**
**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py:109-139`

This function normalizes paths in **3 ways**:
```python
normalized = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")  # Strategy 1: strip + forward-slash
allowed.add(normalized)
allowed.add(rel(filepath))                         # Strategy 2: project-root relative
if resolved_base is not None:
    relative = _relative_to_base(...)             # Strategy 3: base-relative
    if relative is not None:
        allowed.add(relative)
```

**All three representations are added to the same set** to support loose matching later.

---

### 2. **`_file_in_allowed_scope` (still exists)**
**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py:142-151`

This normalizes paths in **2 ways**:
```python
normalized = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")        # Strategy 1
if normalized in allowed_files:
    return True
return rel(filepath) in allowed_files                   # Strategy 2
```

---

### 3. **`_absolutize_review_path` (still exists)**
**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py:39-44`

This normalizes to **absolute resolved paths**:
```python
def _absolutize_review_path(file_path: str, *, project_root: Path) -> str:
    candidate = Path(file_path)
    if candidate.is_absolute():
        return str(candidate.resolve())
    return str((project_root / candidate).resolve())
```

---

### 4. **Additional Normalization Found**
There are **at least 2 more normalization strategies** in the review system:

**a) In `_normalize_allowed_files` (context_holistic/mechanical.py:14-27):**
```python
file_path = raw.strip().replace("\\", "/")
```

**b) In `gather_mechanical_evidence` (context_holistic/mechanical.py:56-60):**
```python
normalized_file = (
    filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    if isinstance(filepath, str)
    else ""
)
```

**c) In `_normalize_file_path` (prepare_batches.py:26-41):**
More specialized: strips quotes, checks for extensionless filenames, validates against canonical extensionless names set.

---

### 5. **Current Path Abstraction Layer**
There **is** a centralized path API, but it's **only for absolute/relative conversion**, not for the kind of normalization needed in the review system:

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/file_paths.py` and `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/paths_api.py`

**Exported functions:**
- `rel(path)` - returns project-root-relative paths with normalized separators
- `resolve_path(filepath)` - resolves to absolute
- `normalize_path_separators(path)` - converts `\` to `/`

But **no unified type or abstraction** for the review system's specific needs.

---

### 6. **Key Problem**
The concern is **still valid**: The review system uses at least **5 different normalization patterns**:
1. `.strip().replace("\\", "/")` (basic strip + forward slash)
2. `rel(filepath)` (project-root relative)
3. `_relative_to_base(filepath, resolved_base)` (custom base relative)
4. `_absolutize_review_path()` (full absolute resolution)
5. `_normalize_file_path()` (specialized validation)

Each location reinvents the wheel, and the **allowed files set** deliberately stores **multiple representations of the same file** to enable loose matching in `_file_in_allowed_scope()`.

---

### 7. **No Canonical File Identity Type**
There is **no dedicated `FileIdentity` or similar type** to represent a file with multiple valid representations. The system relies on:
- `set[str]` to store multiple forms of the same file
- Loose equality checks in `_file_in_allowed_scope()` using two different normalization strategies

This is **fragile and error-prone** — subtle bugs can easily occur when a new normalization strategy is needed or when one is inconsistently applied.

## Summary Report: `lang` Type Annotations in Review System

Based on my thorough research of the desloppify review system, here are the findings:

### Current Type Annotation Status

**`lang` is still typed as `object` or `Any` throughout the review system** - the concern is valid and remains unaddressed.

#### Files with Problematic Type Annotations:

1. **Intelligence/Review Layer (highest concern):**
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py` (lines 111, 233, 356): `lang: object`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/helpers.py` (line 27): `lang: Any`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/selection.py` (line 51): `lang: Any`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py` (line 493): `lang: object`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/_context/structure.py` (line 14): `lang: Any`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/selection.py` (line 26): `lang: object`
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/orchestrator.py` (lines 41, 51): `lang: object`

2. **App Commands/Review Layer:**
   - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py` (line 610): `lang: object`

### The `getattr()` Pattern Problem

Multiple files use `getattr(lang, ...)` without declaring what attributes they need:

1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py:117`: `getattr(lang, "zone_map", None)`
2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/selection.py:160`: `getattr(lang, "complexity_map", None)`
3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/selection.py:31`: `getattr(lang, "zone_map", None)`
4. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/selection.py:313`: `getattr(lang, "review_api_surface_fn", None)`
5. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context.py:143`: `getattr(lang, "review_module_patterns_fn", None)`

Additionally, code directly accesses `lang.file_finder`, `lang.zone_map`, `lang.dep_graph`, `lang.dep_graph` without type safety.

### Positive Finding: Protocol Exists in Framework Layer

**Good news:** A Protocol has been created, but ONLY in the framework layer:

- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/runtime.py` (line 121)
- **Name:** `LangRuntimeContract`
- **Scope:** Defines interface for detector phases only
- **Properties covered:**
  ```python
  config: LangConfig
  zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
  dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None
  complexity_map: dict[str, float]
  review_cache: dict[str, Any]
  review_max_age_days: int
  detector_coverage: dict[str, DetectorCoverageRecord]
  coverage_warnings: list[DetectorCoverageRecord]
  ```

**However:** This Protocol is NOT used in the review system. Review functions use raw `object`/`Any` instead of this contract.

### Migration Signals: Partial Contract Exists

- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_signals/migration.py` (line 14)
- **Name:** `MigrationLangConfig` (Protocol)
- **Scope:** Migration-specific attributes only
- **Properties:**
  ```python
  migration_mixed_extensions: set[str]
  migration_pattern_pairs: list[tuple[str, re.Pattern[str], re.Pattern[str]]]
  ```

This is a minimal Protocol but only covers migration-specific needs.

### The Missing Link

**What's needed but doesn't exist:**
- A comprehensive `ReviewLangProtocol` or `ReviewContextLangProtocol` that declares all attributes needed by the review intelligence system
- Attributes accessed without declaration:
  - `zone_map`
  - `complexity_map`
  - `review_module_patterns_fn`
  - `review_api_surface_fn`
  - `file_finder`
  - `dep_graph`
  - `review_low_value_pattern`

### Conclusion

The concern is **fully valid**. The review system:
1. ✗ Uses `lang: object` / `lang: Any` everywhere
2. ✗ Does `getattr(lang, ...)` without declaring expected protocol
3. ✗ Directly accesses attributes without type safety
4. ✓ Has infrastructure (Protocol exists in framework)
5. ✗ But doesn't use it in review layer

The codebase would benefit from extracting a `ReviewLangProtocol` that documents what the review intelligence layer actually needs from language implementations.

## Summary Report: Plan Reconciliation Exception Handling

Based on my thorough review of the plan reconciliation code, here's what I found:

### Current State: GOOD NEWS - Exception swallowing has been addressed

The concern about "silent exception swallowing in plan reconciliation" has been resolved. Here's the detailed breakdown:

---

### 1. **Primary Location: `scan_workflow.py` - `_reconcile_plan_best_effort()` (lines 491-574)**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`

This is where plan reconciliation is called after a scan. **Exception handling is GOOD - NOT SILENT:**

```python
except _PLAN_SYNC_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
    runtime.state["plan_sync_failed"] = True
    logger.warning("Plan reconciliation skipped due to runtime error: %s", exc)  # ✓ LOGGED
    _record_scan_warning(...)
    
except _PLAN_SYNC_UNEXPECTED_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
    runtime.state["plan_sync_failed"] = True
    logger.warning("Plan reconciliation skipped due to unexpected error: %s", exc, exc_info=True)  # ✓ LOGGED WITH TRACEBACK
    _record_scan_warning(...)
```

**Key improvements:**
- Exceptions are typed (not bare `except Exception`)
- Two exception categories: expected (`_PLAN_SYNC_EXCEPTIONS`) and unexpected (`_PLAN_SYNC_UNEXPECTED_EXCEPTIONS`)
- Both have `logger.warning()` calls - NOT silent
- Unexpected exceptions include `exc_info=True` for full traceback
- Both record user-facing warnings via `_record_scan_warning()`
- Exception is NOT swallowed - error state is recorded in `runtime.state["plan_sync_failed"]`

---

### 2. **Plan Modules: `desloppify/engine/_plan/**`**

Reviewed all files in the _plan package:
- `/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile.py` - NO bare `except Exception` patterns
- `/desloppify/engine/_plan/persistence.py` - Specific exception types with logging:
  ```python
  except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
      print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex})...")  # ✓ LOGGED
  except ValueError as ex:
      print(f"  Warning: Plan invariants invalid ({ex})...")  # ✓ LOGGED
  except OSError as backup_ex:
      _log_backup_failure(backup_ex)  # ✓ LOGGED via logger.debug()
  except OSError as ex:
      print(f"  Warning: Could not save plan: {ex}")  # ✓ LOGGED
  ```

- `/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations.py` - Pure mutation functions, no exception handling needed
- `/desloppify/engine/_plan/auto_cluster.py` - No exception handling (pure computation)
- `/desloppify/engine/_plan/stale_dimensions.py` - No exception handling (pure computation)

---

### 3. **Secondary Location: `override_handlers.py` (lines 46-60)**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`

One `except Exception:` exists but it's NOT silent:

```python
try:
    state_mod.save_state(state_after, state_file)
except Exception:
    try:
        save_plan(plan_before, plan_path)  # Rollback attempt
    except Exception as rollback_exc:
        print(colorize("Warning: failed to rollback...", "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)  # ✓ LOGGED
    raise  # ✓ EXCEPTION IS RE-RAISED (not swallowed)
```

**Analysis:**
- Exception is caught, but **re-raised** at the end - not swallowed
- Inner rollback attempt has error handling with stderr output
- This is intentional transactional logic, not silent swallowing

---

### 4. **Test Code** (NOT production issues)

Two `except Exception:` blocks found in test files:
- `/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_treesitter.py` (test fixture - ignored)
- `/desloppify/languages/python/tests/test_py_smells.py` (test cases - ignored)

These are test code testing detector behavior, not production code.

---

## Conclusion

**The concern has been addressed.** There is **no silent exception swallowing** in plan reconciliation:

1. ✓ Main reconciliation in `scan_workflow.py` has typed exceptions with logging
2. ✓ Plan persistence has specific exception types with user-facing messages
3. ✓ The one bare `except Exception:` in `override_handlers.py` is intentional transactional rollback that re-raises
4. ✓ All logged exceptions include context (error messages, and in one case, full tracebacks)
5. ✓ Failed reconciliation is marked in state (`plan_sync_failed` flag) for visibility

**No changes needed** - exception handling is production-ready.

## Summary: State Typing Status in Desloppify

### The Good News: StateModel TypedDict EXISTS

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py` (lines 151-170)

The `StateModel` TypedDict is comprehensively defined with:

**Required Fields:**
- `version: int`
- `created: str` (timestamp)
- `last_scan: str | None`
- `scan_count: int`
- `overall_score: float`
- `objective_score: float`
- `strict_score: float`
- `verified_strict_score: float`
- `stats: StateStats`
- `findings: dict[str, Finding]`
- `subjective_integrity: SubjectiveIntegrity`
- `subjective_assessments: dict[str, SubjectiveAssessment]`

**Optional Fields:**
- `scan_coverage: dict[str, ScanCoverageRecord]`
- `score_confidence: dict[str, Any]`
- `scan_history: list[ScanHistoryEntry]`
- `concern_dismissals: dict[str, ConcernDismissal]`
- `plan_sync_failed: bool`
- `scan_warnings: list[dict[str, str]]`

It's properly exported in `__all__` and available at the public API via `desloppify/state.py`.

### The Mixed Reality: PARTIALLY Adopted, Not Comprehensive

**Statistics:**
- Functions using `state: StateModel` → **69 occurrences**
- Functions using `state: dict[str, Any]` → **64 occurrences** (still substantial)
- Untyped `state: dict` → **Many more** in the codebase

### Modules That Have Adopted StateModel (Good):
✓ `/desloppify/state.py` (public facade) - e.g., `score_snapshot(state: StateModel)`
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py` - Definition + helpers
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_state/scoring.py` - e.g., `get_overall_score(state: StateModel)`
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` - e.g., `remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, ...)`
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py` - Loading/saving
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_plan/auto_cluster.py` - e.g., `auto_cluster_findings(plan, state: StateModel)`
✓ `/desloppify/engine/_plan/stale_dimensions.py` - All functions use `StateModel`
✓ `/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py` - e.g., `_compute_risk_flags(state: StateModel, ...)`
✓ `/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/reminders.py` - All reminder functions typed
✓ `/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/dimensions.py` - `_analyze_dimensions(..., state: StateModel)`
✓ `/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/action_engine.py` - `supported_fixers(state: StateModel, ...)`

### Modules Still Using `dict[str, Any]` (Problem Areas):

**Review/Import Flow (11 functions spread across 7 files):**
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic.py` - 7 functions
- `/desloppify/app/commands/review/import_cmd.py` - 8 functions
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py` - 5 functions
- `/desloppify/app/commands/review/import_helpers.py` - 3 functions
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py` - 2 functions
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/issue_history.py` - 2 functions

**Other Areas:**
- `/desloppify/engine/concerns.py` - 7 functions (e.g., `_open_findings(state: dict[str, Any])`)
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/remediation_engine.py` - 5 functions
- `/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py` - 2 functions
- `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/runtime.py` - 1 function
- `/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/mechanical.py` - 1 function

### The Defensive Coding Pattern Still Present

Example from `/desloppify/engine/concerns.py` (lines 74-90):

```python
def _open_findings(state: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Return all open findings from state."""
    findings = state.get("findings", {})  # <- Defensive .get()
    return [
        f for f in findings.values()
        if isinstance(f, dict) and f.get("status") == "open"  # <- Defensive checks
    ]

def _extract_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's findings."""
    for f in findings:
        det = f.get("detector", "")  # <- Defensive .get()
        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}  # <- Defensive isinstance
```

This pattern is pervasive: defensive `.get()` chains with defaults, combined with `isinstance()` checks. Even though the structure is defined, the code doesn't trust it.

### Bottom Line

**The concern was valid but has been partially addressed:**

1. **TypedDict exists** - `StateModel` is well-defined and comprehensive
2. **Core modules adopted it** - ~69 functions in critical paths use it (scoring, planning, narrative generation)
3. **Not universally applied** - ~64 functions still use `dict[str, Any]`, mainly in review/import flows and concerns generation
4. **Defensive coding persists** - Even typed functions often add `.get()` defaults and `isinstance()` guards (which is reasonable for data resilience)

The typing is **inconsistent but improving**, with better coverage in the core engine than in peripheral concerns and review workflows. Full adoption would require migrating the review/import and concerns modules to use `StateModel` consistently.

```
   171→        key=lambda item: (
   172→            subjective_score_value(item),
   173→            str(item.get("id", "")),
   174→        )
   175→    )
   176→    priority_ids = {id(item) for item in priority_subjective}
   177→    subjective_tail = [
   178→        item for item in subjective_items if id(item) not in priority_ids
   179→    ]
   180→    mechanical_items = [
   181→        item for item in items if not _is_subjective_queue_item(item)
   182→    ]
   183→    if not mechanical_items:
   184→        return list(priority_subjective) + subjective_tail
   185→
   186→    interleaved: list[dict[str, object]] = []
   187→    subjective_cursor = 0
   188→    for index, item in enumerate(mechanical_items, start=1):
   189→        interleaved.append(item)
   190→        if index % every_n == 0 and subjective_cursor < len(priority_subjective):
   191→            interleaved.append(priority_subjective[subjective_cursor])
   192→            subjective_cursor += 1
   193→
   194→    if subjective_cursor < len(priority_subjective):
   195→        interleaved.extend(priority_subjective[subjective_cursor:])
   196→    interleaved.extend(subjective_tail)
   197→    return interleaved
   198→
   199→
   200→def item_explain(item: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:
   201→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   202→
   203→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   204→        return {
   205→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   206→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   207→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   208→            "policy": (
   209→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   210→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   211→            ),
   212→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   213→        }
   214→
   215→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   216→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   217→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   218→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   219→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   220→    if is_subjective:
   221→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   222→    elif is_review:
   223→        ranking_factors = [
   224→            "tier asc",
   225→            "confidence asc",
   226→            "review_weight desc",
   227→            "count desc",
   228→            "id asc",
   229→        ]
   230→    else:
   231→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   232→    explain = {
   233→        "kind": "finding",
   234→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   235→        "confidence": confidence,
   236→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   237→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   238→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   239→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   240→    }
   241→    if is_review:
   242→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   243→    if is_subjective:
   244→        explain["policy"] = (
   245→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   246→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   247→        )
   248→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   249→    return explain
   250→
   251→
   252→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   253→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   254→    for item in items:
   255→        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
   256→            # Count each member at its own tier
   257→            for member in item.get("members", []):
   258→                tier = int(member.get("effective_tier", member.get("tier", 3)))
   259→                counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   260→            continue
   261→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   262→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   263→    return counts
   264→
   265→
   266→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   267→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   268→    if not available:
   269→        return None
   270→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   271→
   272→
   273→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   274→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   275→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   276→    for item in items:
   277→        if group == "file":
   278→            key = item.get("file", "")
   279→        elif group == "detector":
   280→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   281→        elif group == "tier":
   282→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   283→        elif group == "cluster":
   284→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   285→            key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
   286→        else:
   287→            key = "items"
   288→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   289→    return grouped
   290→
   291→
   292→__all__ = [
   293→    "build_finding_items",
   294→    "choose_fallback_tier",
   295→    "inject_subjective_slots",
   296→    "item_explain",
   297→    "item_sort_key",
   298→    "tier_counts",
   299→    "subjective_score_value",
   300→    "group_queue_items",
   301→]
   302→
```

```
    26→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    27→class QueueBuildOptions:
    28→    """Configuration for queue construction and tier selection behavior."""
    29→
    30→    tier: int | None = None
    31→    count: int | None = 1
    32→    scan_path: str | None = None

    48→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    49→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    50→
    51→    items: list[dict]
    52→    total: int
    53→    tier_counts: dict[int, int]

    60→
    61→def _apply_plan_order(
    62→    items: list[dict],
    63→    plan: dict,
    64→    *,
    65→    include_skipped: bool = False,
    66→    cluster: str | None = None,
    67→) -> list[dict]:
    68→    """Reorder items according to the living plan.
    69→
    70→    1. Items in ``queue_order`` appear first, in that order.
    71→    2. Remaining items keep their mechanical sort.
    72→    3. Skipped items are appended last (or excluded).
    73→    4. Each item is annotated with plan metadata.
    74→    """
    75→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    76→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    77→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
    78→    overrides: dict = plan.get("overrides", {})
    79→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    80→    active_cluster = plan.get("active_cluster")
    81→
    82→    # Build lookup
    83→    by_id: dict[str, dict] = {}
    84→    for item in items:
    85→        by_id[item["id"]] = item
    86→
    87→    # Annotate items with plan metadata
    88→    for item_id, item in by_id.items():
    89→        override = overrides.get(item_id, {})
    90→        if override.get("description"):
    91→            item["plan_description"] = override["description"]
    92→        if override.get("note"):
    93→            item["plan_note"] = override["note"]
    94→        if override.get("cluster"):
    95→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    96→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    97→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    98→                "name": cluster_name,
    99→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
   100→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", [])),
   101→                "sibling_ids": cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []),
   102→            }
   103→
   104→    # Split into ordered, remaining, skipped
   105→    ordered: list[dict] = []
   106→    ordered_ids: set[str] = set()
   107→    for fid in queue_order:
   108→        if fid in by_id and fid not in skipped_ids:
   109→            ordered.append(by_id[fid])
   110→            ordered_ids.add(fid)
   111→
   112→    skipped_items: list[dict] = []
   113→    remaining: list[dict] = []
   114→    for item in items:
   115→        item_id = item["id"]
   116→        if item_id in ordered_ids:
   117→            continue
   118→        if item_id in skipped_ids:
   119→            skipped_items.append(item)
   120→        else:
   121→            remaining.append(item)
   122→
   123→    # Assign queue positions
   124→    result = ordered + remaining
   125→    if include_skipped:
   126→        result = result + skipped_items
   127→
   128→    for pos, item in enumerate(result):
   129→        item["queue_position"] = pos + 1
   130→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
   131→            item["plan_skipped"] = True
   132→            skip_entry = skipped_map.get(item["id"])
   133→            if skip_entry:
   134→                item["plan_skip_kind"] = skip_entry.get("kind", "temporary")
   135→                skip_reason = skip_entry.get("reason")
   136→                if skip_reason:
   137→                    item["plan_skip_reason"] = skip_reason
   138→
   139→    # Filter to cluster if requested
   140→    effective_cluster = cluster or active_cluster
   141→    if effective_cluster:
   142→        cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster, {})
   143→        cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   144→        if cluster_member_ids:
   145→            result = [item for item in result if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   146→
   147→    return result
   148→
   149→
   150→def _item_matches_tier(item: dict, tier: int) -> bool:
   151→    """Check if an item (or any of its members for clusters) matches a tier."""
   152→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
   153→        return any(
   154→            int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) == tier
   155→            for m in item.get("members", [])
   156→        )
   157→    return int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == tier
   158→
   159→
   160→def _action_type_for_detector(detector: str) -> str:
   161→    """Look up the action_type for a detector from the registry."""
   162→    try:
   163→        from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
   164→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   165→        if meta:
   166→            return meta.action_type
   167→    except ImportError:
   168→        return "manual_fix"
   169→    return "manual_fix"
   170→
   171→
   172→_ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   173→
   174→
   175→def _collapse_clusters(items: list[dict], plan: dict) -> list[dict]:
   176→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items."""
   177→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   178→    if not clusters:
   179→        return items
   180→
   181→    # Build mapping: finding_id → auto-cluster name
   182→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   183→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   184→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   185→            continue
   186→        for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", []):
   187→            fid_to_cluster[fid] = name
   188→
   189→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   190→        return items
   191→
   192→    # Collect members for each cluster, preserving order
   193→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   194→    non_cluster_items: list[dict] = []
   195→
   196→    for item in items:
   197→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   198→        if cname:
   199→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   200→        else:
   201→            non_cluster_items.append(item)
   202→
   203→    # Build cluster meta-items
   204→    result: list[dict] = list(non_cluster_items)
   205→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   206→        # Don't collapse singletons — show them as individual findings
   207→        if len(members) < 2:
   208→            result.extend(members)
   209→            continue
   210→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cname, {})
   211→        detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   212→        action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
   213→        # Derive action_type from the actual cluster action, not just the detector
   214→        if "desloppify fix" in action:
   215→            action_type = "auto_fix"
   216→        elif "desloppify move" in action:
   217→            action_type = "reorganize"
   218→        else:
   219→            action_type = _action_type_for_detector(detector)
   220→            # If detector says auto_fix but cluster has no fix command, it's really refactor
   221→            if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify fix" not in action:
   222→                action_type = "refactor"
   223→
   224→        # Use stored description but patch the count if it differs from visible members
   225→        stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
   226→        total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   227→        if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
   228→            # Replace the stored count with the visible count
   229→            summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
   230→        else:
   231→            summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} findings"
   232→
   233→        primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
   234→        if not primary_command:
   235→            primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cname} --count 10"
   236→
   237→        meta_item: dict = {
   238→            "id": cname,
   239→            "kind": "cluster",
   240→            "action_type": action_type,
   241→            "summary": summary,
   242→            "members": members,
   243→            "member_count": len(members),
   244→            "primary_command": primary_command,
   245→            "cluster_name": cname,
   246→            "cluster_auto": True,
   247→            "confidence": "high",
   248→            "detector": detector,
   249→            "file": "",
   250→        }
   251→        result.append(meta_item)
   252→
   253→    result.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   254→    return result
   255→
   256→
   257→def build_work_queue(
   258→    state: StateModel,
   259→    *,
   260→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
   261→) -> WorkQueueResult:
   262→    """Build ranked queue items + tier metadata."""
   263→    resolved_options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   264→
   265→    status = resolved_options.status
   266→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   267→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   268→    try:
   269→        subjective_threshold_value = float(resolved_options.subjective_threshold)
   270→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   271→        subjective_threshold_value = 100.0
   272→    subjective_threshold_value = max(0.0, min(100.0, subjective_threshold_value))
   273→    requested_tier = (
   274→        int(resolved_options.tier) if resolved_options.tier is not None else None
   275→    )
   276→
   277→    finding_items = build_finding_items(
   278→        state,
   279→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   280→        status_filter=status,
   281→        scope=resolved_options.scope,
   282→        chronic=resolved_options.chronic,
   283→    )
   284→
   285→    all_items = list(finding_items)
   286→    if (
   287→        resolved_options.include_subjective
   288→        and status in {"open", "all"}
   289→        and not resolved_options.chronic
   290→    ):
   291→        subjective_items = build_subjective_items(
   292→            state,
   293→            state.get("findings", {}),
   294→            threshold=subjective_threshold_value,
   295→        )
   296→        for item in subjective_items:
   297→            if scope_matches(item, resolved_options.scope):
   298→                all_items.append(item)
   299→
   300→    all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   301→
   302→    # Apply living plan ordering if provided
   303→    if resolved_options.plan:
   304→        all_items = _apply_plan_order(
   305→            all_items,
   306→            resolved_options.plan,
   307→            include_skipped=resolved_options.include_skipped,
   308→            cluster=resolved_options.cluster,
   309→        )
   310→
   311→    # Collapse auto-clusters into meta-items (unless drilling into a cluster)
   312→    should_collapse = (
   313→        resolved_options.collapse_clusters
   314→        and resolved_options.plan
   315→        and not resolved_options.cluster
   316→        and not resolved_options.plan.get("active_cluster")
   317→    )
   318→    if should_collapse:
   319→        all_items = _collapse_clusters(all_items, resolved_options.plan)
   320→
   321→    if (
   322→        requested_tier is None
   323→        and resolved_options.include_subjective
   324→        and status in {"open", "all"}
   325→        and not resolved_options.chronic
   326→        and not resolved_options.cluster
   327→    ):
   328→        all_items = inject_subjective_slots(
   329→            all_items,
   330→            every=resolved_options.subjective_injection_every,
   331→            threshold=resolved_options.subjective_injection_threshold,
   332→        )
   333→
   334→    counts = tier_counts(all_items)
   335→
   336→    selected_tier = requested_tier
   337→    fallback_reason = None
   338→    filtered = all_items
   339→
   340→    if requested_tier is not None:
   341→        filtered = [
   342→            item
   343→            for item in all_items
   344→            if _item_matches_tier(item, requested_tier)
   345→        ]
   346→        if not filtered and not resolved_options.no_tier_fallback:
   347→            chosen = choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier, counts)
   348→            if chosen is not None:
   349→                selected_tier = chosen
   350→                filtered = [
   351→                    item
   352→                    for item in all_items
   353→                    if _item_matches_tier(item, chosen)
   354→                ]
   355→                fallback_reason = (
   356→                    f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open -> showing T{chosen} "
   357→                    "(nearest non-empty)."
   358→                )
   359→        elif not filtered:
   360→            fallback_reason = f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open."
   361→
   362→    total = len(filtered)
   363→    if resolved_options.count is not None and resolved_options.count > 0:
   364→        filtered = filtered[: resolved_options.count]
   365→
   366→    if resolved_options.explain:
   367→        for item in filtered:
   368→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   369→
   370→    available_tiers = [tier for tier, value in counts.items() if value > 0]
   371→    return {
   372→        "items": filtered,
   373→        "total": total,
   374→        "tier_counts": counts,
   375→        "requested_tier": requested_tier,
   376→        "selected_tier": selected_tier,
   377→        "fallback_reason": fallback_reason,
   378→        "available_tiers": available_tiers,
   379→        "grouped": group_queue_items(filtered, "item"),
   380→    }
   381→
   382→
   383→__all__ = [
   384→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   385→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   386→    "WorkQueueResult",
   387→    "build_work_queue",
   388→    "group_queue_items",
   389→]
   390→
```
