I now have a thorough understanding of the system. Here is my analysis.

---

## Work Queue Ranking and Prioritization: Complete Analysis

### Overview

The work queue ranking is a **multi-layer system**: a deterministic mechanical sort produces a default ordering, the living plan can override that ordering, and auto-clustering collapses related findings into single meta-items. The entire pipeline lives in `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/`.

---

### 1. Tiers: The Primary Sort Dimension

Tiers are the single most important factor. Every finding is assigned a tier (1-4) at detection time. The tier is set by each individual detector when it creates a `Finding` dict (via `make_finding()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`, line 141). If missing, the state schema defaults to tier 3 (`schema.py` line 295).

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/common.py` (lines 5-10):

```python
TIER_LABELS = {
    1: "Highest priority (auto-fixable + high-confidence holistic review)",
    2: "Quick fixes (unused vars, dead exports, exact dupes, orphaned files, cross-tool imports)",
    3: "Needs judgment (smells, near-dupes, single-use, small cycles, state sync)",
    4: "Major refactors (structural decomposition, large import cycles)",
}
```

The `Tier` IntEnum in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/enums.py` (lines 48-52) provides the canonical names:

```python
class Tier(enum.IntEnum):
    AUTO_FIX = 1
    QUICK_FIX = 2
    JUDGMENT = 3
    MAJOR_REFACTOR = 4
```

**Special rule for subjective findings**: Any finding from the `subjective_assessment` or `holistic_review` detectors is **forced to effective_tier 4** regardless of its original tier. This is done in `build_finding_items()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` (lines 55-57):

```python
item["effective_tier"] = (
    4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
)
```

---

### 2. The Core Ranking Algorithm: `item_sort_key()`

The ranking is implemented as a **composite tuple sort key** in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` (lines 86-130). Python sorts tuples lexicographically, so earlier tuple positions dominate. There are three distinct sort key shapes depending on the item kind:

#### A. Cluster items (kind == "cluster")

```python
(
    cluster_tier,            # min tier of all members (lowest = most urgent)
    -1,                      # clusters sort BEFORE individual findings at same tier
    action_pri,              # auto_fix=0, reorganize=1, refactor=2, manual_fix=3
    -member_count,           # larger clusters first (negated for desc sort)
    item_id,                 # tiebreaker: alphabetical
)
```

The cluster tier is the **minimum** effective_tier across all member findings (lines 91-100), so a cluster with even one T1 member will sort alongside other T1 items.

#### B. Subjective / subjective_dimension items

```python
(
    effective_tier,          # always 4 (forced)
    1,                       # sort AFTER mechanical items within T4
    subjective_score_value,  # lower score = worse = higher priority (ascending)
    item_id,                 # tiebreaker
)
```

The `1` in position 2 ensures subjective items always come after mechanical T4 items (which get `0` in that position).

#### C. Regular finding items (the common case)

```python
(
    effective_tier,          # 1-4, ascending (T1 first)
    0,                       # mechanical items come before subjective within tier
    confidence_rank,         # high=0, medium=1, low=2 (from CONFIDENCE_ORDER)
    -review_weight,          # only nonzero for review detector findings (desc)
    -count,                  # detail.count, higher counts sort first (desc)
    item_id,                 # tiebreaker
)
```

The `CONFIDENCE_ORDER` dict is defined in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/common.py` (line 12):

```python
CONFIDENCE_ORDER = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
```

#### Summary of sort priority (most to least important):

1. **Tier** (ascending: T1 > T2 > T3 > T4)
2. **Kind discriminator** (clusters at -1, mechanical at 0, subjective at 1)
3. **Confidence** (high > medium > low) -- or for subjective: score value ascending
4. **Review weight** (descending) -- or for clusters: action type priority
5. **Count** (descending) -- or for clusters: member count descending
6. **ID** (alphabetical tiebreaker)

---

### 3. Review Weight: A Special Signal

For findings from the `review` detector, a `review_weight` is computed in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` (lines 49-60):

```python
def review_finding_weight(item: dict) -> float:
    confidence = str(item.get("confidence", "low")).lower()
    weight_by_confidence = {
        "high": 1.0,
        "medium": 0.7,
        "low": 0.3,
    }
    weight = weight_by_confidence.get(confidence, 0.3)
    if detail_dict(item).get("holistic"):
        weight *= 10.0
    return float(weight)
```

Holistic review findings get a 10x multiplier, making them sort dramatically higher within their tier. This weight slots into position 4 of the sort tuple (negated for descending).

---

### 4. Subjective Slot Injection

After the primary sort, there is an interleaving step for subjective items. This prevents subjective work from being starved indefinitely by mechanical findings. The function `inject_subjective_slots()` in `ranking.py` (lines 139-197) works as follows:

- Controlled by `subjective_injection_every` (default 0, meaning disabled) and `subjective_injection_threshold` (default 95.0)
- When enabled, subjective items with scores below the threshold are pulled out, sorted by score (lowest first), and inserted every N mechanical items
- Remaining subjective items (above threshold) are appended at the end

---

### 5. Plan Ordering Override: `_apply_plan_order()`

The living plan can completely override the mechanical sort. This is in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` (lines 61-147).

The plan data (stored in `.desloppify/plan.json`) has a `queue_order` field: an explicit ordered list of finding IDs. The algorithm:

1. **Ordered items first**: Items in `queue_order` appear at the top, in exactly the order specified
2. **Remaining items next**: Items NOT in `queue_order` and NOT in `skipped` keep their mechanical sort order
3. **Skipped items last** (or excluded entirely): Items in the `skipped` dict are appended at the end only if `include_skipped=True`
4. **Active cluster filtering**: If an `active_cluster` is set (via `plan focus`), only members of that cluster are returned

Each item also gets annotated with plan metadata:
- `queue_position` (1-indexed)
- `plan_description` and `plan_note` (from overrides)
- `plan_cluster` (cluster name and metadata)
- `plan_skipped`, `plan_skip_kind`, `plan_skip_reason` (for skipped items)

Users manipulate the `queue_order` through plan subcommands: `plan move` (reposition items), `plan defer`/`plan undefer` (skip/unskip), `plan focus` (filter to cluster).

---

### 6. Auto-Clustering: Collapsing Related Findings

After plan ordering, auto-clusters are collapsed into single meta-items via `_collapse_clusters()` in `core.py` (lines 175-254). The auto-clustering algorithm itself lives in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/auto_cluster.py`.

Auto-clusters group findings by a deterministic `_grouping_key()`:
- **Review findings** group by dimension (`review::abstraction_fitness`)
- **Per-file detectors** (structural, responsibility_cohesion) group by detector+file
- **Needs-judgment detectors with subtypes** group by detector+subtype (`typed::dict_keys::phantom_read`)
- **Pure auto-fix detectors** group all findings together by detector (`auto::unused`)
- **Everything else** groups by detector name

Singleton groups are dropped (minimum cluster size is 2). The collapsed cluster meta-items are then re-sorted using the same `item_sort_key()`, where:
- The cluster's tier is the min of member tiers
- The cluster gets an `action_type` derived from the actual cluster action or the detector's metadata
- Within the same tier, clusters sort before individual findings (discriminator `-1`)

---

### 7. The Full Pipeline (in `build_work_queue()`)

The complete ordering pipeline in `core.py` lines 257-380:

1. **Build finding items**: `build_finding_items()` creates enriched item dicts from raw findings, computing `effective_tier`, `review_weight`, `is_subjective`, `primary_command`
2. **Add subjective items**: Synthetic work items for below-threshold subjective dimensions (always T4)
3. **Mechanical sort**: `all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)` -- the multi-factor tuple sort
4. **Plan override**: `_apply_plan_order()` reorders according to `queue_order`
5. **Cluster collapse**: `_collapse_clusters()` merges related findings into meta-items
6. **Subjective interleaving**: `inject_subjective_slots()` periodically inserts subjective items
7. **Tier filtering**: If a specific tier was requested, filter to just that tier (with fallback to nearest non-empty tier)
8. **Count limiting**: Truncate to requested count

---

### 8. How Detectors Assign Tiers

Tiers are not centrally computed -- each detector assigns its own tier at detection time. Some patterns from the codebase:

- **T1**: Auto-fixable findings (unused imports, debug logs) -- things with an available fixer
- **T2**: Quick fixes (unused vars, dead exports, high-import-count untested modules, some security rules)
- **T3**: Judgment-needed findings (smells, near-dupes, single-use modules, test coverage gaps) -- the default
- **T4**: Major refactors (structural decomposition, large cycles), all review/subjective findings

The `action_type` field in DetectorMeta (from the registry) correlates with but does not determine the tier. The tier is ultimately set per-finding by the detector phase.

---

### Key Takeaways

- **Tier is king**: T1 always beats T2 always beats T3 always beats T4. No amount of confidence or count can promote a T3 item above a T2 item.
- **Within a tier**: confidence (high > medium > low) is the next discriminator, then review weight (for review findings) or count (for others).
- **Subjective findings are sandboxed**: They are forced to T4 and within T4 sort after mechanical items. They can only be promoted via the plan `queue_order`.
- **The plan is the escape hatch**: `queue_order` provides full manual control over ordering, overriding the mechanical sort entirely for any items it contains.
- **Clusters inherit urgency**: A cluster's tier is its most urgent member's tier, so clustering does not hide high-priority items.
